1. INTRODUCTION
ADNOC Group has established the minimum HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements based on ADNOC HSE
Standards and ADNOC Group Medical Fitness Guidelines to facilitate consistent Contractors compliance
across all ADNOC Group contracts.
This document provides quick reference to the requirements stipulated in HSE standards as applicable to the
respective scope of work.
Contractors and their Sub-contractors shall comply with all applicable UAE Federal & Local Regulations,
ADNOC HSE Standards, ADNOC Group supplement procedures, any other relevant Guidelines and ADNOC
Group HSE, Welfare & Medical requirements throughout the contract life cycle.
2. PURPOSE
This document establishes a consistent and unified approach in defining minimum HSE, Welfare and Medical
requirements in all contracts tendered and awarded to various contractors across ADNOC Group.
3. SCOPE
These standardized minimum HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements are applicable to all contracts being
tendered and awarded by ADNOC Group.
While complying with the requirements of this document, in case of any conflict, the requirements of relevant
ADNOC HSE Standards and interpretation by ADNOC Group shall supersede and remain binding. In case of
any conflict or ambiguity on any specific requirement from different standards, the most stringent standards’
requirement shall be applied.
Where any part of the work is not covered by any of the ADNOC HSE Standards, or when the relevant HSE
Standards are considered or found to not precisely covering the work activity, Contractor shall immediately
notify ADNOC of such absence or missing requirements and jointly agree the way forward.
4. DOCUMENT OWNERSHIP
ADNOC Group HSE Function shall be the custodian of this document and shall ensure that the current version
of this document is made available to all concerned.
ADNOC Group HSE Function shall also be responsible for initiating any required changes to the document
for endorsement and approval by the Competent Authority. Any approved changes shall be incorporated by
re-issuing the document at the next revision number.
Any recommendation for the continuous improvement of this document has to be submitted to ADNOC Group
HSE Function, which is the custodian of this document and has the sole right to approve and circulate revisions
for implementation accordingly.

ALARP As low as Reasonably Practicable - Means to reduce a risk to a level, which is as
low as reasonably practicable and involves balancing reduction in risk against the
time, trouble, difficulty and cost of achieving it. This level represents the point,
objectively assessed, at which the time, trouble, difficulty and cost of further
reduction measures becomes unreasonably disproportionate to the additional risk
reduction obtained.
ANSI American National Standards Institute

the parties, exclusive of any allowances whatsoever.

Competent Person A person who has demonstrated the required set of skills and underpinning
knowledge to successfully perform a task to the required standard under different
and changing conditions in the workplace.
Competence The ability to perform a particular job in compliance with industry accepted
performance standards. This encompasses the technical requirements and skill to
perform the job as well as having knowledge and understanding to enable the job
to be carried out successfully under different and changing conditions, and to handle
emergency situations which may possibly occur.

Contracts Those agreements, which relates to the procurement of services and other types of
works issued by the contracts team of any procurement unit.
Contract A person nominated by the user unit (or other units, as applicable) to administer the
Administrator Contract from Award until Closeout.

HAZOP Hazard and Operability; a study that identifies deviations from process boundaries

Health Health is a state of complete physical, mental and social wellbeing and not merely

Healthy Workplace It is a model advocated by the WHO and defined by it as: “A healthy workplace is
one in which workers and managers collaborate to use a continual improvement
process to protect and promote the health, safety and wellbeing of all workers and
the sustainability of the workplace by considering the following, based on identified
needs:
▪ health and safety concerns in the physical work environment;
▪ health, safety and well-being concerns in the psychosocial work
environment, including organization of work and workplace culture;
▪ personal health resources in the workplace; and
▪ ways of participating in the community to improve the health of workers,
their families and other members of the community”.

Licensed Health Health Care Professional (Consultant, Specialist, Medical Practitioners Physician,

Major Accident Major accident means an ‘Occurrence’ such as a emission, fire or explosion
resulting from uncontrolled developments in the course of operation leading to
major, catastrophic or disastrous consequences irrespective of their likelihood and
serious consequences assigned with risk rank 3F (Refer to ADNOC Corporate Risk
Matrix) affecting people, assets, the environment and/or company reputation. The
consequences may be immediate or delayed and may occur outside as well as
inside the operating facility.
There will also be a high potential for escalation Examples of ‘Major Accidents’
would include, but are not limited to:
• Loss of containment of flammable and/or toxic fluids leading to fire,
explosion and/or toxic injury
• Events resulting in structural failure which could lead to further progressive
collapse Loss of stability of mobile offshore installation  Well blowouts
• Ships colliding with offshore installations or onshore jetties used for bulk
loading, explosive, flammable or toxic substances
• Service vessel colliding with or otherwise affecting offshore installations
• Other external hazards affecting offshore and onshore sites e.g.
Accommodation/work barges alongside fixed installations, helicopters and
aircraft, road/marine product tankers.
The definition of ‘Major accident’ specifically relates to “Process Safety” and
excludes ‘Occupational safety accidents’ which have bounded, albeit possibly
severe or catastrophic consequences.
MAH Major Accident Hazard. A hazard that has the potential to result in a ‘Major
Accident’.
MARPOL The International Convention for Prevention of Marine Pollution For Ships
MOC Management of Change.
NCAP New Car Assessment Program
NFPA National Fire Protection Association
NORM Naturally Occurring Radiative Material
Occupational A multidisciplinary field aimed at:
Health (OH)

controlling occupational diseases and injuries by eliminating occupational
factors and conditions hazardous to health and safety at work

PSSR Pre Start-up Safety Review.
PTW Permit to Work
QRA Quantitative Risk Assessment.
Quantitative Risk Assessment; It is a formal and systematic approach of estimating
the likelihood and consequences of hazardous events, and expressing the results
quantitatively as risk to people, the environment, asset or reputation. A structured
approach to assessing the potential for incidents and expressing this potential
numerically. In QRA statistical values are derived for potential loss of life and
damage to resources and environment. Note: These values must never be
interpreted as unavoidable and acceptable losses. It must always be recognised
that the calculated fatality (or loss) figures are based on experience, statistical
failure and incident rates representing an average historical quality of management.
Incident investigations usually show that these ‘historical’ incidents were, with the
benefit of hindsight, quite preventable. QRA is a tool which helps to translate this
hindsight into foresight (planning) in order to assist management in deciding the
best approach and show ways and means (e.g. improved engineering, procedures,
supervision, etc.) to prevent the potential incidents from happening. QRA is not to
be used to justify or encourage risk taking.
Recreation An activity that people engage in during their free time that is recognized as having
socially redeeming values.

(SWL) The maximum load (as determined by a competent person) that an item of lifting

SDS Safety Data Sheet
SHOC Safe Handling Of Chemicals
SIL Safety Integrity Level; Safety integrity level (SIL) is defined as a level of risk-
reduction provided by a safety function, or to specify a target level of risk reduction.
In simple terms, SIL is a measurement of performance required for a safety
instrumented function (SIF). SIL is a statistical representation of the reliability a SIS
requires, and is capable of, when a process demand occurs. SIL is the measure for
the reliability of a SIS, and a given SIL level relates to a specific range of associated
PFD and RRF values as outlined in IEC 61508
SIMOPs Simultaneous Operations (SIMOPS) are activities that take place at the same time
and meet the following conditions:
• Activities that are located in the same area; and
• Activities that could directly or indirectly potentially affect the safe performance of
the another activity (e.g. Breaking Containment and Hot Work)

6.1 POLICIES AND STRATEGIC OBJECTIVES
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall perform the work complying with the applicable Federal and Local Regulations, ADNOC
HSE Policies, Standards, Guidelines, ADNOC Group company supplement procedures and any Guidelines
as applicable.
The Contractors’ HSE Policy and sub-ordinate policies shall include but not limited to;
▪ HSE Policy and Strategic Objectives signed by the Top management
▪ No Smoking Policy
▪ Drug and Alcohol Policy
▪ STOP Work Authority
Contractors shall establish sub-ordinate policies such as Drug and Alcohol policy, No Smoking Policy etc.
Contractor policies shall be consistent with the ADNOC policies.
Contractors shall review and update their HSE Policy based on the level of risk, emerging new legal
requirements, organizational changes, changes in organization’s mandate and Management direction.
Contractors shall establish HSE programmes with clear performance targets to prevent losses and minimise
waste. HSE programs with clear performance targets are to be subjected to ADNOC Group approval.
Contractors shall submit necessary declarations with respect to Incident reporting, HSE Performance, ADNOC
LSRs, etc. as required by ADNOC Group.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1

ADNOC Classification: Internal
6.2 CONTRACTOR LEADERSHIP VISIBILITY AND COMMITMENT
Minimum requirements
Visible presence of Senior Management of Contractors at the work place demonstrates a positive HSE culture.
They shall actively participate in HSE & Welfare walkthroughs, inspections and audits at planned intervals as
agreed with the ADNOC Group. This demonstrates leadership commitment and enables an interactive two-
way dialogue between the workforce and Senior Management to address any concerns raised by the
Contractor and their Sub-contractor personnel in an effective and efficient manner.
Contractors shall also demonstrate visible commitment by personal engagement in HSE activities such as
trainings, reward and recognition, inspections, audits etc.
Contractors shall empower employees to express their concern on unsafe act and unsafe conditions. Line
Management shall be responsible to address such concerns. Such concerns and responses shall be
documented.
Contractors leadership shall ensure Managers and Workers are empowered and obliged to “STOP and
Intervene” when a risk associated with an activity is considered unacceptable, or if the execution of a task
does not meet the stipulated requirements.
6.3 CONTRACTOR SUPERVISION
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall ensure continuous and competent supervision at all times to enable effective implementation
of HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements.
In addition to job specific skills, Supervisors must possess the following minimum skills to oversee the effective
implementation of HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements at site as part of their HSE accountability:
▪ Safe System of Work (WMS/PTW, Method Statement, Risk Assessment etc.)
▪ Behaviour Based Safety approach
▪ Identification of hazards and conduct risk assessment
▪ Awareness on basic welfare measures
▪ Certification requirements for personnel and equipment
▪ Incident reporting and Emergency Response Preparedness
▪ Awareness of ADNOC HSE requirements applicable to Scope of Work under their supervision
▪ Knowledge of HSE Plan requirements
▪ Hazards & required controls pertaining to the Scope of Work
Contractors shall ensure that employees shall not work alone without adequate supervision under any
circumstances.
Contractors shall ensure sufficient resources (i.e. manpower, equipment, infrastructure, funds and supplies)
are available to ensure the effective implementation of the HSE Management System, Strategic Objectives
for continuous improvement. It is also required to ensure staff levels/numbers are sufficient to meet the
manpower requirements of all HSE-critical roles and activities.
6.4 LEGAL & OTHER REQUIREMENTS
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall comply with all applicable relevant UAE Federal laws & Regulations, Ministerial Orders,
Ministerial Resolutions, National and International standards, ADNOC HSE Standards as well as Laws and
Regulations of any other location where ADNOC Group conduct activities including any that may be introduced
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 15 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
at a later date. Contractors shall be fully compliant with any changes or amendments to UAE laws and
regulations. The applicable legal register shall be developed and made available.
Laws and regulations include but not limited to:
• Federal Law No. (8) of 1980 as amended by Federal Law No (8) of 2007
• Federal Law No. (24) of 1999 for protecting and development of the environment
• Ministry Order No.(32) year 1982 regarding the determination of preventative methods and measures for
protection of workers from the risk work
• Law No. (21) of 2005 for the Waste Management in the Emirates of Abu Dhabi
and any amendments thereto for the above laws and regulations.
Contractors shall obtain fresh water requirement for Camps, Accommodation, Offices, etc. from government
authorized sources only such as ADDC water sources (either by ADDC temporary water connection to fulfil
their total water demand or procure only from ADDC Tanker filling stations or any other location of emirate
government agencies). Drilling of ground water wells by contractors is prohibited.
Contractors management shall be accountable for any violation to Safety Rules, Safe Work Practices and
applicable government laws/regulations by their employees.
Contractors shall implement requirements of latest versions of Business Continuity Readiness Guidelines for
UAE Organizations [AE/SCNS/NCEMA 7002:2020 Version II March 2020] including pandemic specific
directives or standing instructions from relevant UAE government authorities, ADNOC pandemic specific
guidelines and protocols such as COVID-19, as applicable, to reduce the risks and minimize the impact on
people and operations.
6.5 ADNOC LIFE SAVING RULES
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC Life Saving Rules Standard, HSE-GA-ST11 for details and necessary
compliance.
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall ensure that all their personnel shall be familiar and compliant with ADNOC Life Saving Rules
(LSR) and work accordingly. Personnel shall be made aware of the consequences of not following the above
rules and actions in case of non-compliance. Group HSE recognition & Accountability framework and
Contractor HSE Performance Management Mechanism shall be applied for any non-compliances and
violations.
Contractors shall demonstrate commitment and adherence to the LSRs at all times, without exception. The
LSRs do not replace the need to adhere to ADNOC HSE Policy and any other ADNOC HSE Standards. They
rather reinforce safety requirements for all work activities that have the highest potential to cause serious
injuries and/or fatalities that require additional focus and strict adherence at all times.
ADNOC Life Saving Rules include:
1. Bypassing Safety Controls
2. Confined Space
3. Driving
4. Energy Isolation
5. Hot Work
6. Line of Fire
7. Safe Mechanical Lifting
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 16 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
8. Toxic Gas
9. Work Authorization
10. Working at Height
Some of the techniques that all Managers and Supervisors are encouraged to follow for maintaining the
visibility of LSRs on daily basis are; Toolbox Talks, Safety Meetings, Pre- job planning, review of the Risk
Assessment, Post-Job Reviews, Observations, Walkthroughs and Interventions etc.
6.6 ADNOC GROUP HSE RECOGNITON AND ACCOUNTABILITY
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC Group HSE Recognition and Accountability Framework for details
and necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
HSE Recognition / Reward Scheme
Contractors need to encourage their workforce through HSE Recognition/Reward program, which recognises
Best HSE performance by individuals or teams, based on approved criteria. Also establish and implement a
program for encouraging and recognising safe behaviours as part of Behaviour Based Safety approach.
In addition, there has to be a mechanism to address consequences on individuals for HSE violations/ non-
compliances.
Contractor shall also implement HSE incentive program as stipulated by the ADNOC Group.
HSE AccountabIlity
Contractors shall adhere to ADNOC Group HSE Recognition and Accountability Framework requirements that
are stipulated from time to time. Any deviations or violations are dealt with the requirements of the framework.
Suitable consequence measures shall be applied based on the nature of violation/ Non-compliance in line with
just culture.
Contractors shall establish their own Recognition and Accountability mechanism in line with ADNOC Group
relevant requirements to ensure effective integration and implementation.
6.7 HSE MANAGEMENT SYSTEM
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Management System standard, HSE-GA-ST02 for details and
necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Contractors shall have an HSE Management System (HSEMS) that aligns with international standards while
meeting ADNOC HSE requirements. It is preferred that Contractors have the ISO certifications such as ISO
45001, ISO 14001 as part of their HSE Management System. However, contractor HSE Management System
shall be sufficient to provide adequate controls and barriers for the risk profile of the project and work being
carried out.
While it is not mandated that Contractors HSE Management System should be identical to that of the ADNOC
Group, it should exhibit the same degree of application and assurance of safe operations.
Contractors shall comply with ADNOC HSE Standards requirements, ADNOC Group HSE Policies,
Procedures, and Manuals within the HSE Management System. Where required, Contractor shall develop
interface documents considering key aspects in the systems interface process between Group Company and
Contractors, which shall be approved by Group Company.
Where a Contractor intends to subcontract any of their activities, the Contractor shall have a demonstrable
robust sub-contractor HSE Management System, associated programme in place, and implemented.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 17 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
ADNOC HSE Management System has Six (06) Fundamental Areas namely
1. Leadership & Employee participation
2. Planning,
3. Support,
4. Operations,
5. Performance Evaluation
6. Continual Improvement
HSE MS Primarily focus on twenty One (21) elements
1. Leadership & Commitment
2. Policy
3. Organization, Roles, Responsibilities and Authorities
4. Consultation and Participation of Employees
5. Risk & Opportunities – Assessment & Management
6. Legal Requirements and Other Requirements
7. Planning Actions to Address Risk & Opportunities
8. HSE Objectives and Planning To Achieve Them
9. Resource & Competence
10. Awareness
11. Communication
12. Documented Information
13. Operational Planning & Control
14. Management of Change
15. Procurement
16. Contractors & Suppliers
17. Emergency Preparedness & Response
18. Compliance Audit
19. Management Review
20. Incident Nonconformity and Corrective Action
21. Continual Improvement
6.8 SUB-CONTRACTOR MANAGEMENT
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall engage only ADNOC prequalified / approved sub-contractors. It is the Principal Contractor
accountability to cascade the ADNOC Group applicable HSE and welfare requirements to their Sub-
contractors and provide continuous oversight to verify compliance with the same.
Contractors shall fully integrate their Sub-contractors into the activities and processes included in the work
site HSE Plans (e.g. orientation, training, hazard management, incident management, equipment
maintenance, emergency preparedness and response, welfare management and HSE performance
measurement).
Contractors shall actively monitor their Sub-contractors HSE performance, integrate it into their overall HSE
performance and conduct periodic audits to verify compliance. In addition, Sub-contractors must be aware of
consequences for non-compliance and mismanagement.
The Contractors shall inform ADNOC Group during the tender period if they wish to hire sub-contractors for
the execution of the contract. Contractor shall be fully responsible and accountable for the HSE & Welfare
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1

ADNOC Classification: Internal
performance of their sub-contractors, and ensure Sub-contractors deliver their scope in compliance with
ADNOC HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements.

the issuing process of security pass. These mandatory HSE training requirements shall include:
(a) HSE Induction including site specific induction and welfare expectations
(b) Toxic Gas including H2S (for all CONTRACTORS working at onshore & offshore sites)

LSRs and the potential consequences for non-compliances. Records of Induction sessions shall be
maintained.
Entry of Contractors employees into the facility are restricted and controlled. Contractors shall issue the
Identification (ID) Cards to the contractor employees only after completion of HSE induction.
6.10 HSE & HAZARD COMMUNICATION
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-OS-ST27 for details on Hazard Communication
and necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements
HSE Communication
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 19 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Contractors shall ensure effective HSE communication and consultation with all personnel involved in the work
including their Sub-contractors. Contractors shall conduct Toolbox meetings prior to the start of work, Site
HSE meetings, HSE workshops, HSE campaigns, Senior Management HSE site visit meetings as defined in
the HSE Plan.
Contractors shall prepare a schedule for HSE meetings, training and campaigns, etc. and this program is to
be reviewed and approved by ADNOC Group.
Contractors shall also participate in HSE meetings, HSE & Welfare campaigns conducted by ADNOC Group.
Hazard Communication
Contractors shall ensure that their employees working under ADNOC jurisdiction /facility/construction
site/project site comply with the requirements of Hazard communication Standards as applicable to their
activities.
Contractors Hazard communication must encompass all risks pertaining to HSE including Occupational risks.
Hazards can be communicated by using a number of techniques depending upon the type of hazard and intent
of communication. These techniques include: (i) Labelling (ii) SDS (Safety data Sheet) (iii) Safety Signs (iv)
Safety Markings (v) Acoustics and Illumination.
Contractors’ personnel who are required to interpret labels, SDS (Safety Data Sheet), safety signs and
markings, shall be provided with appropriate training for the nature their work or potential exposure.
All safety colours shall be selected and designed in line with the latest version of ISO 3864 series of standards.
Various geometric shapes such as circle, square, triangle etc. are used at workplaces to communicate different
types of hazard messages and alerts.
The safety signs shall be suitable for operation in external climatic conditions of a maximum of 60ºC. The
contractor (through its supplier or otherwise) shall demonstrate that the selected materials are suitable for
such applications with a minimum of maintenance.
The safety signs shall be designed to withstand the wind pressures experienced at the installation site in
accordance with the recommendations and design calculations given in BS EN 1991-1-4:2005.
6.11 SHORT Service Employee Program (SSE)
Minimum requirements
Where applicable and as determined by ADNOC Group, Contractors must ensure that a Short Service
Employee (SSE) Program is established based on the principle of managing the risks associated with new
comers (employees new to particular activity/operations) in the site activities. This will be a proactive effort to
ensure that new comers coming to work from different backgrounds are provided with adequate awareness
on hazards and precautions to be taken while executing works at site.
In applicable cases, SSE Program evaluation will form part of the bid process and be subject to ADNOC Group
endorsement and approval.
An SSE process provides for identification of inexperienced personnel so that others may 1) take extra care
in their presence, 2) provide assistance, and 3) provide additional oversight. An SSE process also allows an
organization to implement additional controls when the ratio of SSEs to the total number of personnel in the
workforce is above a designated level.
Define an SSE as an employee with less than three (3) months of satisfactory experience in current job scope,
or less than two (2) years’ service in industry
▪ Provide an immediate and easy means to visually identify a SSE (e.g. specific colour for hard hats or
coveralls, hardhat sticker)
▪ Identify tasks that an SSE is not authorized to perform (e.g. operate certain types of equipment) and
environments that an SSE is not authorized to work within (e.g. working alone)
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 20 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ Assign a designated mentor to every SSE and defines their roles and responsibilities
▪ Utilize an SSE tracking system to maintain and report an accurate count of the number of SSEs in the
workforce on a monthly basis
▪ Establish a threshold for the maximum number of SSEs relative to the total number of personnel in
the workforce based on associated risk
▪ Require mitigation plans to be implemented when the established SSE threshold has been surpassed
▪ Provide a formal means of assessing an SSE's knowledge and skills
▪ Incorporate a training plan that focuses on improving the knowledge and skills of the SSE
▪ Define criteria for retaining or removing SSE status based upon achievement of established criteria
▪ Maintain documentation for assessment and auditing purposes
6.12 HSE INCIDENT REPORTING & INVESTIGATION
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE standard, HSE-GA-ST04 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall have an incident reporting system in line with ADNOC HSE Standards and Group Company
requirements. Contractor shall notify all incidents including those related to their Sub-contractors; work related
and non-work related, Near misses, unsafe acts, unsafe conditions, and High Potential Incidents (HIPO) etc.
by the fastest means of communication available and investigate all incidents. Contractors shall comply with
the requirements of ADNOC Incident Notification, Investigation & Reporting Standard including the definitions
for various types of incidents.
ADNOC Group approved format shall be utilized for reporting incidents and submitted within the specified
period. Contractor shall report incidents, which occur on any premises including ADNOC Group's, the
contractors or other third party locations where work or services are being carried out on ADNOC Group's
behalf.
Contractors shall not allow any person involved in serious incident to leave the country until the completion of
the investigation without prior approval from the ADNOC Group.
Contractors shall ensure that the corrective actions are developed and completed within the agreed target
time period as per the ADNOC Group requirements.
Contractors shall create awareness for relevant employees on the requirements of this Standards and
maintain the records of Training & Awareness.
Contractors shall align their Incident Reporting and Investigation process with the requirements of this
Standard. Contractors shall ensure that trained and competent staff are available for Incident Investigation.
When a Group Company is contractually a contractor to another Group Company, Incidents (during
execution of services) shall be reported in HSE Performance of Group Companies receiving the services
(Client GC) at their premises and NOT to GC providing Services (Contractor GC) as it will be regarded as a
Contractor under this arrangement and hence, the Client GC will be maintaining the man-hours and recording
the incident under its HSE performance / KPIs. Once the work is suspended by the Client GC, the HSE
Performance shall be recorded with the Contractor GC.
Reportable Contractors shall be responsible for:
▪ Reporting Incidents and Near Misses in timely manner.
▪ Proactively reporting unsafe acts/ behaviour and/or unsafe conditions to prevent incidents.
▪ Participate and cooperate in incidents investigation as and when required.
▪ Aligning their Incident Reporting and Investigation process with the requirements of this Standard
▪ Ensuring that trained and competent staff is available for Incident Investigation.
▪ Ensuring that injury/ illness cases are managed as per ADNOC HSE Standards
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 21 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ Activation/ deactivation of the worksite access to the injured/ ill employees
▪ Accountable for CICPA cancellation for the permanently unfit employees
Case Management - Occupational health case management is the process of assessing, planning, facilitating
and evaluating an employee’s occupational health needs in order to achieve overall employee health in a cost-
effective and efficient manner. Contractors shall comply with the requirements of ADNOC HSE-OH-ST04
Case Management and Rehabilitation Standards.
6.13 HSE AUDIT AND INSPECTION
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-GA-ST09 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Contractors shall ensure effective compliance verification is in place through audits and inspections.
Contractors shall ensure Recommendation Tracking System is in place to ensure that all recommendations
and actions arising from hazard and aspects analyses and reviews are recorded and closed-out.
Contractors shall maintain an effective control process to ensure that only personnel who have received
adequate training shall lead audits. Audit findings are recorded, prioritized and corrective actions are identified
with assigned action parties and targeted completion dates. Audit findings are tracked and followed-up until
final close-out, Best Practices and lessons learned are shared with all locations/assets as appropriate.
Contractors shall note that the ADNOC Group has the full right to audit contractors' performance for
compliance with HSE and Welfare requirements at all stages of contract and that the contractor shall be
required to implement ADNOC Group instructions in this regard at the cost of the contractor.
Contractors shall provide senior personnel to participate in Company led integrated HSE audits of the
operations contracted to them.
Contractors shall establish and maintain documentary evidence for key aspects
▪ Job descriptions (including key contractor staff)
▪ Contractor HSE staff arrangements
▪ HSE or HSEMS manual
▪ Contract HSE requirements
▪ HSE performance indicators and statistics ( Incidents data for last year and year to date with
investigation reports of significant incidents)
▪ HSE Plan
▪ HSE Targets and Objectives
▪ HSE inspection and audit program
▪ HSE competencies and training matrix (HSE training requirements for all staff and contractor per job
positions)
▪ Incident follow-up status records
▪ HSE meeting structure and schedule
▪ Minutes/follow-up reports of HSE meetings
▪ HSEIA and other risk management studies
Contractors mobilization shall be conditional upon receipt of an acceptable HSE Plan based on the level of
HSE risk and suitable interface arrangements to ensure compliance with the company HSEMS. All high-risk
contracts shall have a documented demonstration as to how the risks are reduced to ALARP. For major
contracts this demonstration can be in the form of an HSE Case.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 22 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
An HSE inspection/audit program shall be in place for all contracts to monitor effective implementation of
HSEMS and interfaces between the contractor and ADNOC Group.
Contractors is required to develop a HSE audit and inspection plan in order to identify the type, frequency,
location during execution of the works. Audits and inspections shall be conducted as outlined in their Group
Company approved HSE Plan by the HSE staff and others as deemed necessary.
Contractors shall implement the ADNOC Group HSE Assurance program as defined by the Group Company
in line with applicability matrix as stipulated in Contractor HSE Management standard (e.g. ABC) on yearly
basis and measured on half yearly basis or as per the frequency defined by ADNOC Group against the set
KPIs.
HSE audit and inspection program shall cover support vessels, camp accommodation and welfare aspects by
utilizing well defined checklists as applicable to the activities.
Contractors shall submit the results of audits and inspections to the ADNOC Group representative. Any
serious non-conformance identified during the audit process shall be subjected to the ADNOC Group HSE
consequence management process.

6.14 HSE PERFORMANCE MANAGEMENT
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standards, HSE-GA-ST08 and HSE–GA-ST05 for details and
necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall provide “HSE Performance data” to ADNOC Group Representative on a frequency specified
by ADNOC Group. This shall include as a minimum the data required by ADNOC HSE Performance Monitoring
& Reporting Standard and Contractor HSE Management Standard.
Contractors shall ensure that ADNOC Group defined HSE performance measures (Leading and Lagging
indicators) are recorded and submitted as per the stipulated frequency.
Contractors overall performance shall be evaluated considering the rating achieved in these HSE measures
during the execution of the activities.
Contractors shall have an annual HSE Assurance program /HSE KPIs, follow ADNOC Group programs as
applicable, and integrate their Sub-contractors in this process.
Contractors also submit their HSEMS internal audits, statistics, HSE Statement, HSE Assurance Program,
and HSE Statistics to ADNOC Group representative including their subcontractors.
Where Contractors hires Sub-contractor(s) for part(s) of the scope delivered to ADNOC Group, any non-
compliances or incidents incurred by Sub-contractor will be accounted by ADNOC Group against the
respective Contractor. Hence, Contractor is accountable to integrate and engage their respective Sub-
contractors in effective management of HSE and Welfare.
Contractors shall have a transparent and well-documented system for communicating the HSE performance
(including their Sub-contractors) to ADNOC Group and submit regular reports as per the stipulated timeline in
an agreed format between ADNOC Group and the Contractor.
Contractors shall comply with the ADNOC Group Contractor HSE Performance Management Mechanism and
is obligated to adhere to the outcomes set out thereafter.
Contractors shall establish HSE performance management mechanism to their Sub-contractors similar to
ADNOC Contractor HSE Performance Management Mechanism to ensure effective integration among
ADNOC Group, Contractors and Sub-contractors as Contractors are accountable for their Sub-contractors
HSE performance.
ADNOC Group can perform periodic HSE inspections/audits and in extreme circumstances, exercise the right
to curtail or suspend work due to HSE and Welfare non-compliances/violations.
Contractors shall not be entitled for any claim or compensation from ADNOC Group for the expenses/losses
incurred as a result of suspension of their activities due to HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements related
non-compliances/violations.
Refer APPENDIX 2 of this document for Contractor HSE Performance Management Mechanism
associated details and attachments
6.15 MOBILIZATION / START-UP OF THE WORK
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall ensure readiness prior to mobilization to the work site that all the HSE and Welfare
requirements/ arrangements are in place.
Contractors shall submit the evidence of civil defence and other camp associated government approvals along
with ADNOC Group acceptance of the camp prior to mobilization of their employees to camp / work site.
The ADNOC Group shall conduct pre-mobilization/mobilization audits and pre-execution audits as per the
ADNOC Group requirements and the recommendations of such audits shall be implemented by the contractor.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1

ADNOC Classification: Internal
If there are any major findings, which might endanger HSE & Welfare, the Contractor shall postpone the
mobilization until such findings are satisfactorily closed.
Prior to mobilization and commencement of any work, Contractor shall provide a written confirmation stating
that both contractor and their Sub-contractors fulfil the requirements of the contract by submitting a
commitment letter signed by the Contractor Senior Management. Copy of which shall be submitted by the
Contractor to sites / fields / project locations Contract Administrator.
Mobilization shall cover the kick-off meeting, Initial & final HSE compliance visits (correction of deficiencies),
preliminary Contractor site visit, submission of ADNOC Group HSE procedures to Contractor if necessary,
site HSE pre-job briefing etc.
Contractors HSE plan specific to the scope of work and the project HSE organization shall be approved by
the ADNOC Group Contract Administrator prior to mobilization.
6.16 EMERGENCY RESPONSE
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standards, HSE-CE-ST01, HSE-CE-ST02 and HSE-CE-ST03 for
details and necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Contractors shall ensure effective Emergency Response Preparedness is in place prior to conducting the
activities at work site and personnel are aware of the response procedures.
Contractors shall develop and implement an Emergency Response Plan in compliance with ADNOC Crisis &
Emergency Response & Crisis Management Standards and ensure integration with Facility Emergency
Response Plan where applicable.
Contractors shall comply with SIMOPS controls as applicable.
Prior to mobilization, Contractors shall develop and submit a project site-specific interface Emergency
response plan including camp facilities (bridging document) which shall be approved by the ADNOC Group.
A site-specific emergency response plan shall include incident management guidance for the chemical
hazards scenario.
Contractors shall provide awareness training to all employees including their Sub-contractors on ADNOC
Group’s Emergency response procedures.
Contractors shall provide the ADNOC Group with the 24 hr. Emergency contact numbers and emergency
facilities locations prior to arrival on ADNOC Group premises.
Prior to commencement of the activities and during the execution of works, frequent drills and exercises shall
be conducted to ensure proper implementation of the plan. They shall participate actively in the emergency
exercises conducted by the ADNOC Group.
Contractors shall maintain the register of employees’ next of kin details, which shall be made available to
ADNOC Group representative upon request.
Contractors shall provide support for mobilization of Contractor medical resources for any emergency on
ADNOC Group fields in case requested.
Emergency contact details of Company and Contractor shall be displayed at prominent places and to be
communicated.
Contractors shall conduct emergency exercises as per the agreed plan to test the effectiveness of the plan
and address recommendations in a timely manner. Contractor shall ensure actions are tracked and closed in
a timely manner as applicable.
Contractors shall ensure their personnel participate in ADNOC Group Emergency Exercises as and when
conducted. Contractors shall develop Accommodation Camp Emergency Response Plan and conduct
emergency exercises to test the effectiveness of the plan.

6.17 HSE BUDGET
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall allocate sufficient budget to ensure implementation of ADNOC HSE Standards, ADNOC
Group HSE procedures, HSE Management System requirements, Contractual HSE, Welfare and Medical
requirements in line with the scope of work.
6.18 HSE ORGANIZATION
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall allocate sufficient and competent HSE resources depending on the volume of work / size of
the project and proportionate to the workforce to satisfy its obligation as per the contractual HSE and welfare
requirements.
Contractors must in all cases demonstrate appropriate management of risk specific to the nature of the work,
through the manpower planning, selection and appointment of competent HSE resources sufficient to manage
all work activities. This is irrespective of the number of personnel at any given site and subject to approval in
all cases
Throughout the contract period, all HSE accountability remains with the Contractor. Hence, it is expected that
the Contractor (e.g. Major EPC) establish their own HSE organization suitable to the scope of work
(independent / not inclusive of their Sub-contractors HSE staff) to ensure full and effective implementation of
ADNOC HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements. Hierarchy of such organisation shall cover various positions
such as HSE Manager, HSE Engineer, HSE Officer, Environmental Engineer, Road Safety Coordinator,
Hygiene Officer, as relevant to the scope of work, and obtain approval from the ADNOC Group Contract
Administrator prior to commencement of the relevant work phase. In addition, Sub-contractors shall establish
their own HSE organization suitable for the scope of work. EPC Contractor shall provide appropriate resources
for the HSE Team to monitor various project locations.
For high risk/critical activities, the requirement of HSE personnel shall be mandated by the ADNOC Group as
deemed appropriate, considering the span of control of activities, geographical work location and irrespective
of the overall number of workforce.
Ratio of HSE personnel proportionate to the number of workforce at site in general for all the
contractors is expected to be as stipulated below:

Contractors shall submit a project HSE organization chart with detailed number of HSE personnel as part of
the HSE plan for ADNOC Group review and approval.
Contractors shall ensure HSE personnel reporting lines are to the highest authority in the area of activities to
enable empowerment for effective implementation of HSE and Welfare requirements.
CVs of HSE Personnel shall be submitted to Company for review 30 days prior to mobilization. Contractor
nominated HSE personnel shall be subject to selection process (interview and assessment) and deployed
only upon ADNOC Group approval. Contractors shall have no right to contest the assessment and selection
process of ADNOC Group.
Contractors must demonstrate that HSE personnel appointed for the management of Sub-contractors have
appropriate monitoring authority for implementation of HSE and Welfare requirements.
Contractors HSE personnel should have relevant experience in scope of work, which they are going to
undertake (e.g. brownfield activities, commissioning etc.). Contractors HSE personnel who did not pass the
ADNOC Group competency assessment and were not accepted, shall not be proposed for the same contract
within the following 6 months.
Contractors shall not relocate, remove or replace any of their ADNOC Group approved HSE personnel without
obtaining written concurrence of the ADNOC Group representative, and subject to providing documentary
evidence and justification for the request for removal or relocation. Contractors shall provide suitable
replacement duly approved by the ADNOC Group for the position that has become vacant within the time
frame specified by ADNOC Group.

In the event of short-term absence of the Contractor’s HSE personnel from the site for whatever reason (e.g.
health-related, family reasons etc.), it is the Contractor responsibility to ensure suitable replacement is
provided immediately with prior approval from ADNOC Group so as to ensure continuity of HSE coverage.
Contractors shall propose HSE personnel as per the criteria stipulated below for different types of HSE roles.
Any deviation from the eligibility criteria in terms of qualifications or experience shall be adequately justified
and requires written approval from the ADNOC Group Corporate HSE Management.
For small contracts e.g. 3-5 personnel to work at site, contractor corporate HSE representative may cover site
requirement and frequency for such cases and to be agreed with Contract Administrator.

6.19 RISK MANAGEMENT
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standards, HSE-RM-ST01 and HSE-OS-ST03 for details and
necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Contractors shall be responsible for ensuring that all activities performed within ADNOC Group operating
facilities/projects in the course of carrying out their contractual requirements are managed in accordance with
the requirements of the standard and reported to the Contract Administrator/holder when applicable.
Contractors shall have a risk assessment procedure, conduct risk assessments for the activities involving
relevant stakeholders based on the degree of the risk associated with the assigned activities and ensure all
the personnel involved in the activities are aware of the control measures that should be implemented.
A risk assessment shall be performed early on in the pre-mobilization phase and should incorporate findings
from the initial risk assessment conducted by ADNOC Group during the planning phase. Any
recommendations that arise from the risk assessment exercise shall be tracked by both parties and closed
prior to mobilization.
The Job Safety Analysis is carried out to ensure that content and location of a work scope are clearly defined,
potential hazards of the work site and each of the tasks or activities to be performed are identified, the
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 32 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
significance of hazards is adequately assessed and sufficient control measures are defined to reduce the risks
to as low as reasonably practicable.
JSA Levels
JSA Level 1: Standard Hazard and Control Form (SHCF)
For General Work permits, SHCF (Appendix 3 of the standard) shall be utilized to identify hazards and controls.
This SHCF assists to manage hazards for the proposed work. SHCF provides a list of potential hazards
however, specific activity related hazards (and controls) can be added as applicable.
The SHCF shall be attached to the permit by the Performing Authority (PA). In addition to PTW
details, the PA shall identify the following on SHCF:
(i) Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) requirements;
(ii) Applicable Life Saving Rules (LSRs);
(iii) Applicable hazards (aligned with the work scope) and required controls.
Controls in SHCF can also be marked as pre-requisite by Area Authority (AA) which allows verification of
implemented controls prior to permit authorization.
Such attachments detailing the required controls shall then be reviewed by the Area Authority (AA)
Prior to permit authorization.
Prior to issuance of the permit, the Permit Issuer (PI) shall confirm that all controls (including prerequisite
controls) as per the requirements of the permit and JSA are in place and effective.
JSA Level 2: Hazard Management Form (HMF)
For Critical and Hot Work permits, SHCF (Appendix 3 of the standard) and Hazard Management Form (HMF-
Appendix 4) shall be utilized. The SHCF shall be used as input for identifying specific hazards and controls
related to the job steps and situational conditions and support preparation of the HMF.
The HMF shall be attached to the permit by the Performing Authority (PA). In addition to PTW details, the PA
shall identify the following on HMF:
(i) Major work steps;
(ii) Applicable hazards (aligned with the work scope) and its location;
(iii) Required controls.
Controls in HMF can also be marked as pre-requisite by Area Authority (AA) which allows verification of
implemented controls prior to permit authorization. AA shall ensure personnel responsible for control
implementation are identified in the HMF.
Such attachments detailing the required controls shall then be reviewed by the Asset Approval
Authority (AAA) before it proceeds to Area Authority (AA) for authorization.
Note: Group Companies using established and approved Formal Risk Assessment may attach the Formal
Risk Assessment in the place of the Level 2 JSA.
Formal Risk Assessment
For a work activity or a work scope, the Integrated Work Activity Planning (IWAP) team determines whether a
Formal Risk Assessment shall be required.
The objective of the Formal Risk Assessment is to identify all hazards and threats, assess the Risks of
identified Hazards for Worst-Case Scenarios using the ADNOC RAM, apply the principles of Hierarchy of
Controls and further determine the controls necessary to reduce the risks to ALARP.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 33 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
If, as a result of this assessment, it is found that the risks posed by the identified hazards cannot be reduced
to ALARP then one of the following approaches shall be adopted:
(i) Work scope and / or method should be redefined with a new Risk Assessment undertaken for the
revised/modified work scope;
(ii) Work should not be undertaken until the hazardous conditions can be removed or a safer method identified
(for example deferring work activities until a total plant shutdown).
Risk Assessment Team Composition
The minimum composition of the Risk Assessment Team shall be based on the highest initial risk level of the
work activity for various risk levels as mentioned below:
Low Risk
▪ Performing Authority (PA) or PA Supervisor
▪ Asset Owner Rep. (Area Authority or his rep.)
Medium Risk
▪ Performing Authority (PA) or PA Supervisor
▪ Asset Owner Rep. (Area Authority or his rep.)
High Risk
▪ Performing Authority (PA) or PA Supervisor
▪ Asset Owner Rep. (Area Authority or above)
▪ HSE Representative
▪ Affected Parties (as applicable)
▪ Fire & Rescue Supervisor (as applicable)
▪ Other Specialists (as applicable)
Note 1: The Risk Assessment shall be initiated by the Performing Authority (PA) or his supervisor.
Note 2: One of the Risk Assessment team members shall meet the competency criteria defined in this
standards to act as a Risk Assessment Team Leader
Review of the risk assessment should involve the following:
▪ Periodic review of identified risk levels/categories which are not yet mitigated or in process of
mitigation to confirm the assumptions made during risk assessment are still valid and identified
controls are functioning as intended. These activities should be carried out by facility/ site team;
▪ Monitoring status of identified risk reduction measures to confirm the plan to manage and mitigate the
risk is progressing. Where alternate options are identified during the review, risk assessment shall be
updated accordingly; and
▪ Monitoring status of existing controls and any short term actions in place until permanent mitigation
measures are implemented
6.20 HSE PLAN
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standards, HSE-GA-ST02, HSE-GA-ST06, and HSE-GA-ST07 for
details and necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 34 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Contractors shall develop HSE plans (Project HSE Plan, Construction HSE Plan, Environment Management
Plan, Welfare management plan etc.) as per the scope of work and in line with ADNOC Group requirements.
It is Contractor’s responsibility to get full understanding of the scope of work and if required, carry out site
visit/site survey to understand the scope in detail prior to Bidding for the tenders to assess the work place
potential hazards and to have clarity on neighbouring facilities, which may have impact on the scope of work.
Commercial variations / claims for any lack of understanding of HSE and Welfare requirements associated
with the Scope of Work will not be entertained after Contract award.
Contractors shall develop HSE Design Philosophy with the project specific Occupational Health, Safety and
Environmental philosophies based on HSE requirements outlined within ADNOC HSE-GA-ST07 and updated
at every stage of the project to suit facility life cycle as applicable
Contractors must have a defined audit/review program as part of their HSE Plan for work including that of their
Sub-contractors. The documented evidence of these audits shall be available to the ADNOC Group together
with the follow-up action taken.
The Project HSE Plan is a document developed at the screening and concept stage and then updated before
being formally issued prior to the commencement of each succeeding stage of project development, when a
closer definition of HSE requirements has been established. The HSE Plan shall be further updated capturing
necessary input from the “Project HSE Philosophy” document as detailed in this Standards, thus ensuring all
HSE requirements for any new project; from the conceptual Prior to Operations stage to (Operations start-up)
and decommissioning / abandonment stage, at the end of facilities lifecycle (HSE Plan).
Contractors shall comply with all the requirements and recommendations of HSE deliverables as stipulate
under various stages of the project in line with ADNOC HSE Standards and ADNOC Group Companies
requirements as applicable.
EPC contractors shall ensure that all fabrication contractors and road haulers have an effective HSEMS. This
shall include full Job Safety Analysis for the critical products they are manufacturing and delivering.
ADNOC HSE MS stipulates 6 Fundamentals with elements and sub elements. A typical HSE plan shall
consider the following fundamentals and elements while developing the HSE Plan to meet the intent of
ADNOC HSE MS expectations as a minimum.
The extent and description of the individual elements would depend on the complexity of the contract.
Fundamental 1- Leadership & Employees’ Participation
▪ Visibility & Commitment
▪ HSE Policy
▪ Proactive in Target Setting HSE KPIs
▪ Code of Conduct
▪ Roles and Responsibilities
Fundamental 2- Planning
▪ Evaluation and Assessment (Risk register/TRAs)
▪ Legal Requirements and Other Requirements
▪ HSE Strategic Objectives
▪ Planning to Achieve HSE Strategic Objectives
Fundamental 3- Support
▪ Resources (General resources/man power/equipment)
▪ HSE Professionals (HSE Organization)
▪ Competence assurance process)
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 35 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ Training (HSE Trainings/Matrix)
▪ Awareness
▪ Standards, Procedures & Work Instructions
▪ Work Management System
▪ Control of Documented Information
Fundamental 4- Operation
▪ Eliminating Hazards & Reducing Risk
▪ Procurement
▪ Contractor and Suppliers (Sub-contractors Management)
▪ Emergency Preparedness & Response
Fundamental 5- Performance Evaluation
▪ Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis & Performance Evaluation(HSE performance evaluations)
▪ Compliance Audit(HSE Audits/Inspections)
▪ Management Review
Fundamental 6- Improvement
▪ Non-Compliance and Corrective Action
▪ Incident Investigation, Reporting and Follow-Up
▪ Continual Improvement
6.21 HSE STUDIES
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standards, HSE-RM-ST02, HSE-RM-ST03, HSE-RM-ST04, HSE-
RM-ST05, HSE-RM-ST06, HSE-RM-ST07, HSE-RM-ST08, HSE-RM-ST09, HSE-RM-St10, HSE-RM-ST11,
HSE-RM-ST12, HSE-RM-ST13, HSE-RM-ST14 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Contractors shall conduct HSE studies as defined by the ADNOC Group and in line with the scope of work at
their own cost and maintain records. In addition shall ensure actions are tracked and closed in a timely manner
as applicable. Contractors shall provide all updated project documents, drawings and all other documents
related to HSE studies under contractor scope of work as part of Engineering HSE Plan and HSE. Contractors
must ensure that the applicable HSE studies have been conducted to the satisfaction as approved by the
ADNOC Group and recommendations of various studies are addressed appropriately. The status of the close
out of all the recommendations shall be submitted to the ADNOC Group representative on periodic basis.
Contractors shall conduct HSEIA with all associated HSE Studies as applicable in line with ADNOC HSE
Standards to demonstrate the design, construction, operations integrity of the project/operation. These
reviews/studies includes but not limited to; HAZOP, HAZID, ENVID, OHID, SIL, QRA, FERA, EERA, ESSA
etc. Contractor shall ensure actions are tracked and closed in a timely manner as applicable.
All the Technical Lead Engineers involved in developing, chairing, facilitating, preparing, reviewing and
presenting HSEIA and HSE studies shall be qualified in Bachelor’s Degree in their respective discipline
(Engineering/Science/Medicine) with a minimum of 10 years of experience in handling HSE risk studies in Oil
& Gas/Chemical/Fertilizer/Petrochemicals or any other hazardous industry.
All Engineers supporting technical leads & involved in developing & preparing HSEIA and HSE Studies shall
be qualified in Bachelor’s Degree in their respective discipline Engineering/Science/Medicine) with a minimum
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 36 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
of 5 years of experience in handling specific disciplines/subjects in Oil & Gas/ Chemical/ Fertilizer/
Petrochemicals or any other hazardous industry.
All contractors undertaking Air Dispersion Modelling (ADM) studies on behalf of ADNOC Group shall be
category a registered with EAD. The ADM study lead shall have minimum experience in undertaking air
modelling studies as stated above under the minimum competency requirements for HSE studies.
Contractors shall ensure that the competency requirements of contractor / sub-contractor HSE discipline
engineers & workshop chairman shall meet the ADNOC HSE standards and the candidature shall be approved
by respective ADNOC Group Corporate HSE.
Contractors shall provide scribe, venue and facilitate Project HSE Reviews (PHSERs) that will be chaired by
ADNOC Group.
Contractors shall conduct reviews/workshops such as HAZID, HAZOP, ISD, SIL ISD, SIL etc. Inherently Safer
Design (ISD) with an independent chairman and submit report to ADNOC Group. CV shall meet ADNOC HSE
Standards and Chairman shall be approved by the respective ADNOC Group Corporate HSE.
Contractors shall ensure actions /recommendation from various HSE Studies conducted are tracked and
effectively closed in a timely manner as applicable.
6.22 MEDICAL REQUIREMENTS
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC Medical Fitness Guidelines, AHQ-HCA-GSA-GID-003-R00-19 for
details and necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements
The CONTRACTOR shall comply (and procure that all SUBCONTRACTORS and each CONTRACTOR
PERSONNEL shall comply) with:
a) all LAWS regarding HSE; and
b) the HSE policies of the COMPANY while at any of the COMPANY’s [PREMISES / SITE] and any
HSE requirements specific to the [WORKS / SERVICES]
The CONTRACTOR shall comply (and procure that all SUBCONTRACTORS and each CONTRACTOR
PERSONNEL shall comply) with all the HSE medical requirements set out in ADNOC Medical Fitness
Guidelines
The CONTRACTOR shall be fully responsible for ensuring and verifying that the medical assessment of each
of the CONTRACTOR PERSONNEL has been conducted in full compliance with the HSE medical
requirements as set out in ADNOC Medical Fitness Guidelines
The CONTRACTOR shall promptly upon request (and in any case by no later than [three (3) DAYS]), provide
the COMPANY and its AFFILIATES, authorised representatives, agents or auditors with the medical reports
and/or certificates of each CONTRACTOR PERSONNEL and any other documents reasonably requested by
the COMPANY and its AFFILIATES, authorised representatives, agents or auditors, as proof of the
CONTRACTOR’s compliance with all LAWS regarding HSE and its HSE-related obligations under this
AGREEMENT.
The CONTRACTOR shall indemnify and hold harmless the COMPANY INDEMNIFIED PARTIES from and
against all losses, liabilities, damages and costs (including legal costs) arising out of or in connection with any
claim by any PERSON against the COMPANY INDEMNIFIED PARTIES in respect of failure by the
CONTRACTOR to comply with its obligations
The declaration of medical fitness is a pre-requisite for any Contractor, Sub-contractor and/or Vendors
personnel visiting or working at site.
Contractors shall ensure Emergency medical care facilities at worksite for their employees in accordance with
government regulations, prior to the start of the job. The contractor emergency medical care also include
arrangement for emergency transportation.
Contractors shall be able to demonstrate that personnel, selected for positions for which Fitness to Work
medical evaluation is required, follow the guidance as laid down in ADNOC Group Medical Fitness
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 37 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Assessment Guidelines. If in the opinion of ADNOC Group and reflected in relevant medical report, any
personnel are not medically fit to work, then ADNOC Group reserves the right to refuse permission for that
personnel.
Contractors shall ensure that all personnel engaged in the work are medically fit for the job to be undertaken,
in possession of valid medical fitness certificate compliant with ADNOC Medical Fitness Examination
requirements Contractor shall provide to ADNOC Group a copy of medical fitness certificate of every employee
as a pre-requisite to be granted access to the worksite.
Contractors shall ensure that a Qualified Occupational Medical Specialist or Medical practitioner with an
experience in Occupational medicine shall perform medical examinations. The medical specialist shall follow
ADNOC Medical Fitness Guidelines criteria and frequency of the assessment.
Depending on the type of exposure, contractor shall develop and implement health surveillance program in
line with this ADNOC Group HSE-OH-ST05 Health Screening & Surveillance. The outcome of health
screening and surveillance shall be fully documented to maintain the confidentiality of health information and
records as per UAE Legal requirement.
Contractors shall ensure at all times that, a current valid Medical Fitness Certificate (MFC) is maintained for
each of its personnel working at or visiting any of the ADNOC Group’s work locations. Any contractor personnel
found non- compliant with the above shall not be permitted to access and/or removed from any ADNOC
controlled site. Contractors are allowed to bring valid medical certificates from another project/contract for the
same job role. They need not perform another medical examination for the new contract for those personnel
for whom there is a valid medical fitness certificate available.
Where an employee has been evacuated from a work location for medical reasons, he shall be thoroughly
examined and an up-to-date MFC issued prior to his return to duty (Return to Work fitness).
Contractors shall ensure medical wastes are disposed in line with relevant regulations through authorised
agencies.
ADNOC Group shall have the right to audit the Contractor’s medical facilities and records as required.
Refer APPENDIX 1 of this document for associated details on Contractors Medical requirements
6.23 OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH REQUIREMENTS
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standards, HSE-OH-ST01, HSE-OH-ST02, HSE-OH-ST03, HSE-
OH-ST04, HSE-OH-ST05, HSE-OH-ST06, HSE-OH-ST08, HSE-OH-ST09, HSE-OH-ST10, HSE-OH-ST11,
HSE-OH-ST-12, HSE-OH-ST13 and HSE-OS-ST28 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements:
Contractor shall develop and implement programs associated with Physical, Biological, Chemical Ergonomic
and Psychological hazards as applicable to their activities.
Contractors shall comply with the following requirements where applicable;
▪ Conduct Occupational Health Risk Assessment, both qualitative and quantitative
▪ Conduct OHRA study as per the methodology provided in HSE-OH-ST03 Occupational Health Risk
Management Standards. OHRA study shall be carried out by a qualified, experienced and
demonstrably competent personnel, who is either appointed [e.g. external consultant] or directly
employed by the Contractor organization
▪ Ensure that OHRA shall encompass all facilities, workplaces and activities to be performed by
contractors, where potentially hazardous agents are present
▪ Ensure OHRA report (with laboratory and exposure assessment reports) shall be accessible and kept
in record for a minimum of 40 years from the date of issue or for the lifespan of the facility, whichever
is the longest

▪ Ensure that the results of OHRA report shall be summarized and documented in the Health Hazard

Contractor shall ensure their personnel are aware of Occupational Health Education & Development
including but not limited to the following aspects:
▪ Contractors shall conduct periodic awareness on the Occupational Health topics - Beat the Heat
Campaign, Personal Hygiene Campaign, Noise awareness, Chemical Handling, Hand Arm and Whole
Body Vibration, Ergonomics and Manual Handling, Radiation Protection, etc.
▪ Contractors shall conduct awareness session on public health issues obesity, cardiovascular diseases
(CVDs), diabetes, smoking etc.
▪ Contractors shall report and investigate occupational illnesses as well as Non-accidental death events
PHYSICAL HEALTH HAZARDS
Contractors shall develop and implement physical agents’ management program, including Noise, Vibration,
Heat Stress, Ionizing and Non-Ionizing, Naturally Occurring Radioactive Material (NORM) to ensure the risks
is controlled and it’s As Low as Reasonably Practicable (ALARP).
CHEMICAL HEALTH HAZARDS
Contractors shall develop chemical hazard management program adopting “cradle to grave” approach in the
chemical management.
The program shall cover:
▪ Chemicals (New or Existing);
▪ Asbestos Containing Material (ACM);
▪ Manmade Fibres (MMF);
▪ Other process and manufactured chemicals. ( e.g. welding fumes)
The chemical hazard management program shall include but not limited to:
▪ Health, Safety and Environment approval of new chemicals,
▪ Evaluating the risks associated with chemical hazards present in the working environment;
▪ Controlling the risks through application of the hierarchy of controls;
▪ Recognition, evaluation, and control of worker exposures to asbestos fibres; and
▪ Recognition, evaluation, and control of worker exposures to man-made fibres.
After delivery of chemical onsite, appropriate communication of hazard(s) posed by the chemical shall be
relayed to ADNOC Group employees and contractors. This shall include the application of the Globally
Harmonized System (GHS) Classification and Labelling of Chemicals and the use of safety data sheets.
Hazard communication includes ensuring training and competency for the end user in using a particular
chemical and recognition of its risk(s)
Contractors shall maintain register of the approved chemicals as applicable
BIOLOGICAL HAZARDS
Contractors shall develop and implement biological hazard management program. The program shall include
but not limited to:
▪ Types of biological hazards

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ Evaluation of risk associated with the identified biological hazards
▪ Control measures to be established to control biological hazard including sampling program
▪ Pandemic preparedness plan
▪ Management of Legionella bacteria in hot and cold water systems
ERGONOMIC HAZARDS & MANUAL HANDLING
Contractors shall develop Office and Industrial Ergonomics Program aimed in identifying, evaluating and
adequately controlling the work tasks and activities that may contribute to the incidence of musculoskeletal-
related disorders.
INDOOR AIR QUALITY
Contractors shall ensure that Indoor Air Quality parameters are within the limits stipulated in the ADNOC
Group Indoor Air Quality Hazards Standards
The Indoor Air Quality Hazards Standards covers indoor environments, which encompass offices, control
rooms, living accommodation, rest areas and other indoor facilities associated with an organization.
PSYCHOSOCIAL HAZARDS
Contractors shall develop Psychosocial Hazard Management program for recognition of risk factors,
evaluation of magnitude of psychosocial risks and control of these risks through appropriate psychosocial
hazard interventions stipulated in ADNOC Psychosocial Hazards Standards.
FATIGUE MANAGEMENT
Contractors shall develop Fatigue Risk Management program which includes the following aspects:
▪ Procedure for Fatigue Risk Management
▪ Recognition, risk evaluation, control (includes recommendation following assessment, institution and
effectiveness)
▪ Training and awareness
6.24 HEAT STRESS MANAGEMENT
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-OH-ST08 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements:
Contractors shall ensure compliance with the UAE labour law with regard to heat stress management and
ADNOC Group requirements.
Contractors shall also implement the mandatory Local requirements for a midday break for people working
under the sun during the summer months. As part of this midday break, workers are banned from working
under the sun or in exposed outdoor areas for specified hours.
Contractors shall actively involve and participate in the Heat Stress campaigns conducted by ADNOC Group,
shall cascade associated message to their employees and comply with all government regulations with respect
to work and rest times.
Contractors shall have an ADNOC Group approved heat stress management program which shall cover but
not limited to:
▪ Adequate mid-day breaks during summer, which shall be in accordance with the UAE Laws,
Regulations and Minister Orders.
▪ Abu Dhabi Ministerial Order or other Emirate equivalent
▪ Provision of cool drinking water accessible to workers
▪ Provision of Air Conditioned rest areas /Cooling shelters /rest shades etc.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 40 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ Thermal Work Limit (TWL) monitoring as a measure of the maximum safe work rate for the conditions
as per Department of Health requirements
▪ Ensure workers with medical condition shall discuss with their Nurse/ Doctor regarding working in hot
environment
For UAE conditions, TWL (in W/m2) values fall into the following three principal categories:
▪ Green Zone (TWL: 140 - 220 W/m2), classified as a low risk, unrestricted zone, where there are no
work limits on self-paced work for educated, hydrated workers;
▪ Amber/Orange Zone (TWL: 115 - 140 W/m2), classified as a medium risk, cautionary zone and
indicating the presence of situations where the prevailing environmental conditions require additional
precautions to be taken
▪ Red Zone (TWL <115 W/m2), classified as a high risk zone in which strict work and rest cycles are
necessary.
▪ The values provide a measure of the maximum safe work rates for the prevailing environmental
conditions. Hence, if TWL is too low, even low rates of work cannot safely be carried out continuously.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1

6.25 WELFARE MANAGEMENT
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standards, HSE-OH-ST06 and HSE-OH-ST07 for details and
necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall comply with UAE labour law, ADNOC Contractor Welfare Management standards and the
welfare requirements as stipulated in their contract by the ADNOC Group. Contractor shall be accountable for
their sub-contractors welfare management.
Contractors shall comply with ADNOC Group welfare requirements regardless of within or outside the ADNOC
Group concession areas as applicable.
Contractors operating a camp shall develop camp welfare management plan and Emergency Response plan.
Contractors shall ensure compliance to minimum requirements for providing and maintaining high quality
welfare conditions for their workforce including their Sub-contractors. Working hours shall be in accordance
with local laws and regulations as a minimum and Contractors shall comply with contractual obligations with
respect to the work hours. Working hours including applicable breaks shall be adopted for specific climatic
conditions.
ADNOC Group HSE function where required jointly with the respective Directorate may conduct adhoc
inspections at Government camps e.g. ICAD as and when advised by Senior Management.
ADNOC Group reserves the right to access any Contractor’s location, during day or night without prior notice
for the purpose of examination or investigation, related to any HSE Incidents or welfare issues and obtain
necessary documents or take samples as required. It shall be noted that Contractors or their employees can
be interviewed either privately or in the presence of witnesses about any matters related to compliance with
welfare provisions.
Contractors shall comply with any amendments or new cabinet decisions that may come into force from time
to time without any claim to ADNOC Group.
All camps, regardless of the state of ownership or extent of lease period, to meet the ADNOC and UAE Fire
and Life Safety Code of Practice requirements as applicable. Contractors shall comply with any amendments
or revisions to the UAE Fire and Life Safety Code of Practice requirements that may come into force from time
to time without any claims to the ADNOC Group.
Contractors shall implement welfare measures and shall be subjected for stringent action in case of any
serious violations or incidents such as strikes in line with Consequence Management Process, and has
potential impact on being invited for future tenders.
Contractors shall engage in ADNOC Group Welfare awareness campaigns and communicate the information
down the line to their employees.
Contractors shall report immediately any welfare issues / concerns/incidents and take immediate actions to
restore the situation to normal.
Contractors shall maintains list of all their Sub-contractors and their camps data and submit the status to the
contract administrator as and when requested.
Contractors shall establish a mechanism to encourage suggestions on welfare aspects and maintain records
of the action taken for those suggestions.
The following mandatory UAE and any location specific (Emirate of UAE, as applicable) legal requirements,
or related to the engagement of workers/personnel for work shall be undertaken by the Contractor
Management as a minimum:
▪ Initial and periodic medical examination at no cost to all employees
▪ Demonstrate that human rights are preserved throughout recruitment, placement, work and return of
their employees by exerting appropriate level of due diligence

and practical advice.
Pest control contractors shall dispose the hazardous waste which include the empty containers of pesticides
/ insecticides as per the TADWEER requirements

storage and disposal of waste materials from the Worksite. Contractor shall develop procedures to ensure

disposed in an environmentally friendly manner and within applicable UAE laws and regulations and shall use

Contractors shall develop Environment Management Plan and comply with ADNOC and TADWEER
requirements for management of construction waste.
Contractors shall comply with ambient noise levels defined in ADNOC HSE Standards HSE-EN-ST02 from
the equipment and activities.

Non Road diesel engine Emission Standards Stage 1/2 or equivalent US EPA Tier 1-3 non-road diesel engine
emission standards at a minimum while a higher such specification is preferred.
Contractors shall comply with discharges shall meet the relevant discharge limits specified in ADNOC
Standard HSE-EN-ST02 Appendix 4. It must be noted that the Abu Dhabi Department of Energy is the current
competent authority for the regulation of wastewater in the Abu Dhabi. The Department of Energy regulations

including the provisions set out in the International Convention for the Prevention of Pollution from Ships

Contractors shall comply with requirements of areas storing liquid materials that are hazardous or have the

Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-EN-ST04 for details and necessary compliance.

Contractors shall comply with UAE Laws, Abu Dhabi Centre of Waste Management (TADWEER) regulations
and ADNOC Waste Management Standard [HSE-EN-ST04] requirements for waste management [from
generation to disposal].
Contractor shall develop waste management plan including classification and strategy and approved by
ADNOC Group.
Contractors shall comply FANR license requirements for handling and disposal of radioactive waste (FANR-
REG-26), FANR-RG-018.
Contractors shall develop waste management plan (WMP) in line with ADNOC HSE Standards for waste
minimization, collection, segregation, and disposal.
Contractors shall comply with HSEIA requirements, EAD guidelines for protection of biodiversity.
Contractors shall comply with GHS standards.
Contractors shall comply with transport of hazardous waste from facilities and must take all necessary
precautions as required by the Regulations of UAE Cabinet, Regulation for Handling Hazardous Materials,
Hazardous Wastes and Medical Wastes, 2002.
Used or expired chemicals shall be transported to ADNOC Hazardous Waste Disposal facility (i.e., BeAAT) in

Appropriate waste disposal standards shall be followed in handling of chemical waste, such as the ADNOC
Waste Management Standards (HSE-EN-ST04, Ref. 32) and applicable Centre for Waste Management
(TADWEER) Regulations.
Contractors shall manage site segregation of waste (hazardous, non-hazardous, recyclable, etc.) according
to ADNOC Group waste management procedure.
Contractors shall utilize the waste disposal agencies authorized by UAE Environmental bodies and ensure
that the chain of custody is maintained. Contractor will be required to supply ADNOC Group with information
such as Safety Data Sheets of chemical and hazardous substances in use.
