Bump Test and Calibration
▪ A bump test and the status of calibration shall be checked before every use of portable gas monitors
in accordance with the manufacturer's recommendation. Records of device testing and maintenance
shall be kept for verification and audit purposes
▪ Calibrations are required on a periodic basis. All sensors regardless of the operational principle, will
drift over a period of time and require adjustments
▪ The frequency of calibration shall be governed as per the manufacturer’s recommendation as
indicated in the instruction manual for the routine test and re-calibration interval
▪ It is recommended that the maximum work limit for the continuous work inside Confined Space should
not exceed more than 2 hours
▪ For any Confined Space entry logging or tally system shall be necessary to check everyone in and
out and to control duration of entry
▪ Exhaust ventilation shall only use air-moving devices approved for use in a hazardous or classified
location when controlling flammable atmospheric contaminants
▪ Rescue plan shall be in place before starting Confined Space entry activity
In case welding activities/hot works inside the confined spaces are required to be carried out, the,
requirements stipulated under cutting, welding and allied processes shall be complied including the fire watch
requirements.

ADNOC Classification: Internal
CSE Observer
▪ A CSE Observer is appointed who understand their responsibility through an adequate training and
will record the entry /exit of the personnel from the Confined Space using Confined Space entry/exit
log sheet
▪ Any change/replacement of the CSE Observer shall be authorized by the PTW Issuer
Contractors shall utilize Confined Space Entry procedure/Permit to Work procedure in conjunction with other
relevant procedures in order to safely perform work inside a confined space.
Periodic testing of the atmosphere in confined space using calibrated gas testing equipment by trained and
authorised personnel is required.
Gas test and monitoring data on the Confined Space Entry permit/certificate must be recorded and use of safe
and reliable communications methods (radios must be intrinsically safe) in confined spaces must be followed.
It shall be ensured that explosion proof lighting is used when natural lighting is not adequate in confined spaces
in hazardous areas.
If confined space is vertical, a retriever tripod complete set is to be provided.
All Entrants, Attendants, Rescue Team members and those preparing confined space risk assessments shall
be trained and competent to work.
When natural ventilation is not adequate, forced ventilation shall be used and such requirement shall be
explosion proof, if utilized in hazardous areas.
6.42 PRESSURE TEST/LEAK TEST
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-OS-ST10 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Contractors shall ensure pressure test/leak test is conducted with prior information to the personnel involved
in the activities and ensure all equipment is fit for purpose and personnel are competent to perform the work.
Contractors shall wherever possible, carryout pressure testing in a permanent dedicated area. Concrete walls
with inspection windows shall shelter such area. When unable to do so, pressure testing shall be supported
by a Risk Assessment and controlled under permit to work procedure.
Contractors shall ensure as a minimum, barriers and warning signs erected at a safe distance around the
area. Contractors must verify that the pressure testing /Leak Testing equipment is certified, maintained, and
operational with calibrated gauges to ensure pressures remain within the specified ranges.
Contractors shall ensure that only trained and competent personnel are involved with High pressure Water
jetting (HPWJ) activity. As a minimum personnel handling and using the HPWJ system should have twelve
(12) months of experience and be trained in emergency first aid. Water jetting equipment and attachments
shall only be used in accordance with manufacturer’s recommendation.
Contractors shall ensure that water jetting equipment shall be not be modified without the approval of the
manufacturer; and shall be maintained by a competent person in accordance with the manufacturer’s
recommendation. Hoses shall be connected with “whip checks”. The main function of the whip check is to
prevent the ends of the hoses from whipping around if the coupling breaks.

ADNOC Classification: Internal
6.43 HAZARDOUS MATERIALS STORAGE AND HANDLING
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-OS-ST12, HSE-OS-ST13 for details and
necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Contractors shall implement all the precautions while handling and storing of hazardous materials.
Contractors shall implement the ADNOC Group procedures and safe work instructions while handling
Hazardous materials & Substances (Gases, Liquids, Solids and Asbestos).
Contractors shall identify all hazardous materials and collect health and safety information such as Safety
Data Sheet (SDS) from the Suppliers. Specific risk assessment in the selection, application and use shall be
carried out based upon the same Safety Data Sheets shall be displayed in storehouses and in prominent work
locations and personnel must be provided with awareness.
Contractors shall ensure packaging, transportation; storage and disposal of hazardous chemicals shall be as
per manufacturer’s recommendations and comply with UAE Laws and regulations.
In order to make sure that workers using hazardous material are well informed of hazards, Contractors shall
implement a procedure to provide instructions for use and minimum required PPE.
Storehouses for hazardous material shall be clearly identified, labelled with adequate warning signs and
appropriate firefighting equipment shall be available in the immediate vicinity.
Storage areas shall be equipped with secondary containment, gas detectors, spill kits, safety showers and
eyewash stations as per the ADNOC Group requirements.
The use of explosive materials shall be strictly restricted to the specialist activities for which they are absolutely
required and have no substitute in Geophysical and Drilling Operations.
All personnel involved in the use, handling and transportation of explosives shall be adequately trained and
qualified to be able to identify all specific hazards associated with those activities.
Requirements to be qualified for working with explosives shall include, but not limited to:
▪ Obtaining a health certificate stating the absence of physical illness, mental illness, psychological
disorder, and fitness to perform duties from the Ministry of Health and the Health Authority of Abu
Dhabi;
▪ Providing a certificate or evidence of training in the handling and transport of explosives, with relevant
work experience covering the previous 24 months;
▪ Providing evidence of positive security check or no previous criminal records; and undergoing a Basic
First Aid training course.
Personnel who participate in the storage, handling, and use of explosives at well operations sites shall meet
the minimum qualifications of API RP 67 or equivalent.
Persons undertaking drilling/loading or shooting operations within the seismic industry shall be qualified,
certified and trained in the following and be under the direct supervision of a person with the following
qualification, certification and training;
▪ A chief blaster/shooter or senior competent blaster/shooter, consistent with his/her seniority and
blasting/shooting experience for the job at hand
▪ Have a valid Blaster/shooter Certificate from local jurisdiction, as applicable and a competence
certificate delivered following examination
▪ Have had a minimum of 6 months' experience as a blaster's helper in the previous 36 months or
passed an examination acceptable to the local jurisdiction or have such other qualifications as may
be acceptable such as certification from a formal training organization
▪ Possess a Certificate of Training in the Transportation of Dangerous Goods if required
▪ Company Seismic Blaster Safety Training or local equivalent (regulatory dependent)
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1

ADNOC Classification: Internal
STORAGE OF EXPLOSIVES
Explosives are very delicate and environmental factors such as temperature and humidity have an impact on
their performance and stability. Explosives shall be kept in accordance with suppliers’ requirements and
recommendations.
General requirements of explosives storage shall include, but are not limited to:
▪ Never keeping primed explosives in storage.
▪ Only temporary storage shall be allowed at operating sites for the specific operations required.
▪ Dedicated storage that is at a safe distance from any hazards.
▪ Detonators and explosives shall be stored separately and a minimum distance of 25 meters shall be
kept between them.
▪ Storing only in their original packaging.
▪ Keeping away from exposure to direct sunlight.
▪ Properly stacking and securing from any accidental impact.
TRANSPORTATION OF EXPLOSIVES
Any transport of explosives shall comply with UAE government regulations and Police requirements dictating
time and quantities allowed.
A journey management plan shall be in place before the start of the journey. The plan has to include, but will
not be limited to:
▪ Selection of safest and most secure route avoiding populated areas and industrial areas
▪ Safe rest stops
▪ Security escort (preferred with uniformed national law enforcement guard)
▪ Emergency response protocol in case of vehicle break down
The vehicle used to transport the explosives shall be fit-for-purpose and well-maintained. The vehicle shall be
equipped with emergency equipment such as a first aid kit and fire extinguishers.
Non-sparking material shall cover any parts of the vehicle which may come in contact with the explosive load.
The vehicle shall have at least one fire extinguisher in the vehicle along with a first aid box.
There shall be no radio equipment fixed inside the vehicle, unless they come equipped with approved
magazines with detonator segregation.
The vehicle shall be protected with at least one security guard. It can never be left unattended.
Vehicles carrying explosives must not stop to refuel or enter a workshop for services.
Explosives and detonators shall be transported in separate Faraday boxes, preferably placed in separate
vehicles altogether.
Only authorized passengers trained in handling explosives may travel with together with the explosives.
All the stored hazardous substances shall have hazard warning labels on the storage container. The purpose
of hazard warning labels is to immediately alert personnel at a facility that hazardous substance in the storage
area have the potential to cause harm to human health and the environment.
These labels shall comply with the following requirements:
▪ All Hazardous material container and package shall be clearly identified and labelled, regardless of
its size or whether it is a temporary or permanent container;
▪ All Hazardous substances stored in a storage container shall have sufficient space available for a
hazard warning label
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 104 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ The information on the hazard warning label shall reflect the information noted in the SDS;
▪ Labels shall be pasted onto each container using a substance durable enough to withstand normal
transportation conditions; and
▪ If the label is worn out on delivery, the handler shall re-label it using an extra copy supplied by the
vendor or shall communicate to the vendor to provide replacement labelling; Labels shall include, as
a minimum, the Globally Harmonized System (GHS) product identifier. All text shall be provided in
English and Arabic
ROAD TRANSPORT
The following requirements shall be applied for the transportation of hazardous substances by road:
▪ Vehicles used to carry drums are drop-sided;
▪ In addition to the vehicle fire extinguisher, an extinguisher appropriate to the load is carried;
▪ In addition to basic PPE requirements, drivers and their helpers are issued with PPE appropriate to
the load being carried, e.g. goggles, chemical handling gloves, etc.
Tanker vehicles carrying flammable / hazardous substances shall have:
▪ An exhaust system forward of the front axle, below the chassis on the driver’s side, with discharge
directed away from the tank on the driver's side of the vehicle or an exhaust system fitted with an
ATEX approved flame/spark arrestor;
▪ Anti-static tyres i.e. conductive, with a maximum resistance of 1 x 106 Ohms;
▪ A master isolation switch outside the cab to isolate all electrical circuits, except the tachograph/ IVMS.
The switch shall be clearly marked in Arabic and English "Electrical Isolation Switch" in red letters on
a white background. The "On" and "Off" positions of the switch itself shall be clearly marked in Arabic
and English. A sign in Arabic and English reading "In Case of an Accident or Fire Put this Switch to
Off" shall be fitted next to the switch. The sign shall be red text on a white background, readable at a
distance of 5m;
▪ Battery terminals that are covered to prevent accidental shorting;
▪ The loading and discharge piping / valving shall be designed in such a way that when in transit, the
flammable substance is only carried within the body of the tank, not within the piping external to the
tank;
▪ Electrical wiring protected by solid draw tubing suitable for bulk vehicles carrying Class 1 petroleum
products;
▪ A fire-resistant shield, screening the engine from the body of the vehicle if the engine protrudes behind
the rear of the cab, or if the engine is open to the rear. The fire screen shall cover any unenclosed
part of the engine above or to the rear of the cab and extend down to the top of the chassis side
members;
▪ At least one means of earthing the tanker for use during loading and unloading operations;
▪ A vacuum relief valve, with suitable flame/spark arrestor, which may be fitted within the manhole;
▪ Drivers shall be equipped with all necessary Safe Handling Of Chemicals (SHOC) and Transport
Emergency Cards (TREM) according to the vehicle load;
▪ Shall be fitted with rollover protection device which automatically shuts down the engine and
isolates the battery. This device shall not get activated at less than 45° to the vertical;
▪ The vent and dumper valves shall be suitable for the appropriate charge/discharge flow rate with
safety relief for pressure/vacuum venting with integral flame trap, mounted on the manhole cover;
▪ Overfill protection and vapour recovery system shall be installed;
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 105 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ All tankers shall be protected from overpressure by being fitted with at least one pressure relief valve
or vent, and all vents shall be fitted with suitable flame / spark arrestors;
▪ All nipples shall be metal instead of plastic;
▪ The exhaust muffler shall be in a horizontal position, equipped with an ATEX approved spark arrestor;
▪ Diesel-powered engines are recommended for tankers, with the air inlet placed at a high level
(preferably above the vehicle’s cab) so that any flammable gas from the tank is unlikely to enter the
engine;
▪ The exhaust system shall be located or shielded such that any spillage of flammable material will not
directly fall onto a hot part of the exhaust;
▪ The electrical system shall be protected by proper siting within the chassis to minimize risk of sparks
occurring, mechanical damage and risk of electrical fires. Cables shall be protected against impact
and heat from the engine and securely fastened to prevent damage. It shall also be insulated and
sized adequately to avoid overheating;
▪ Batteries shall be sited and protected against any leakages of flammable material, and insulated to
prevent any possible sparks;
▪ There shall be at least one means of earthing the tanker during loading and unloading operations.
Tyres and hoses used shall be of anti-static type;
SEA TRANSPORT
The transportation of hazardous substances by sea, and storage on board a vessel, is carried out in
accordance with the International Maritime Dangerous Goods (IMDG) Code. The Code lays down basic
principles; detailed recommendations for individual substances, materials and articles, and a number of
recommendations for good operational practice including advice on terminology, packing, labelling, stowage,
segregation and handling, and emergency response action.
In addition, the following requirements apply:
▪ Drums of chemicals are transported in drum cages specially designed and constructed for that
purpose;
▪ Cargoes of palletized chemicals remain strapped to the pallets;
▪ Damaged pallets are not used and are replaced before lifting is attempted;
▪ Special pallet lifting devices are used to lift pallets from the quayside onto the vessel, and from the
vessel to the platform/rig barge;
▪ All other packaged chemicals are contained in transport or cargo boxes; and
▪ Only lifting equipment with current, valid certification is used
▪ If standards ISO containers or other types of certified lifting appliance are used for the transportation
of hazardous substances, the dispatcher shall ensure that supply labels are conspicuously duplicated
on the door of the container or on the side of the lifting appliance
AIR TRANSPORT
Hazardous substances shall only be transported by air in emergencies. In such cases, advice will be given by
the Chief Pilot who decides if the hazardous substances can be transported in accordance with the
International Civil Aviation Organization Technical Instructions for the Safe Transport of Dangerous Goods by
Air.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 106 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
REQUIREMENT FOR PROPER STORAGE OF HAZARDOUS MATERIALS
▪ In a storage area, hazardous materials having the most risk for causing serious harm shall be
segregated (or separated) from the others. The segregation of substances shall be ensured based on
the hazardous materials compatibility charts;
▪ The hazardous materials storage area shall only be dedicated to storing hazardous substances.
Stored materials shall be properly stacked to minimize the risk of being easily knocked down;
▪ Storage of hazardous materials in open storage area can be accepted for some materials under the
condition that such storage conditions does not contradict with the storage conditions specified in the
SDS. Open storage areas shall be secured, and all stored materials shall be covered with flame-
retardant covers;
▪ Stored materials that can be damaged by exposure to water shall be kept a minimum of 3 inches
above the floor (such as on pallets);
▪ Hazardous materials shall not be stored in alphabetical order because this could result in the storage
of incompatible materials near each other;
▪ If a hazardous material has more than one hazard class, then priority must be given to the highest
hazard class (primary hazard);
▪ Hazardous materials shall be stored in stacks for easy access;
▪ Stack locations shall be clearly identified with signs;
▪ The height of the stacks shall not be from the floor up to the ceiling. Instead, there should be a
minimum of 3 feet between the top of the stacks and the ceiling;
▪ Stacks shall be arranged so they do not obstruct the movement of forklifts and other equipment;
▪ Empty containers and expired materials shall be stored in a separate, designated area, and the area
must be labelled as “Quarantine Area” or “Expired Chemicals”; and
▪ Special outlets shall be located in the storage area that is connected to sumps, which collect the
leaked or spilled hazardous material
6.44 H2S MANAGEMENT
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-OS-ST21 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum Requirements
Contractors shall ensure their personnel to work in an area or field that has the presence of H2S, must be
trained and competent.
Contractors shall comply with H2S Zoning & Hazardous areas classification requirements as applicable. The
Contractor shall provide required respiratory equipment such as EEBA, SCBA & Air loop systems as
applicable to the scope of the activities along with personal toxic detectors to all workers to ensure safe escape
from the location in case of H2S emergency.
Theoretical H2S Zoning Criteria
RED ZONE
Entry Requirements:
▪ A breathing airline or SCBA set shall always be donned and in use when entering the RED zone. For
new projects / facilities, use of breathing airline should be considered over SCBA for RED Zones
▪ JSA shall clearly specify the SCBA/air-line requirements;
▪ Personal H2S detector shall always be worn by all personnel entering the zone;
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ Buddy System;
▪ Access control and personnel tracking;
▪ Authorized and trained personnel only;
▪ H S competence certificate required for entry
2
Evacuation Recovery and Rescue:
▪ At least two independent escape routes out of Red Zone;
▪ At least two muster points in opposite direction in the plant located outside the H2S impacted area;
▪ All escape routes on the facility leading to muster areas where a H2S hazard exists shall be passable
AMBER ZONE
Entry Requirements:
▪ EEBA carried and kept at hand’s reach while entering the Amber Zone.
▪ For those, involved in performing activities requiring opening of process components which contain,
or may contain H2S (intrusive work/breaking containment) shall have the breathing airline apparatus
or SCBA set donned and in use;
▪ Personal H2S detector shall always be worn by all personnel entering the zone;
▪ Buddy System;
▪ Access control or personnel tracking;
▪ Authorized and trained personnel only;
▪ H2S competence certificate required for entry
Evacuation Recovery and Rescue:
▪ At least two independent escape routes out of Amber Zone;
▪ At least two muster points in opposite direction in the plant located outside the H2S impacted area ;
▪ All escape routes on the facility leading to muster areas where a H2S hazard exists shall be passable
by personnel wearing SCBA
YELLOW ZONE
Entry Requirements:
▪ EEBA available at strategic locations and Personnel entering zone shall be aware of the PPE
locations;
▪ Personal H2S detector shall be worn at all times;
▪ Authorized and trained personnel only;
▪ H2S competence certificate required for entry
Evacuation Recovery and Rescue:
▪ At least two independent escape routes out of Yellow Zone;
▪ At least two muster points in opposite direction in the plant located outside the H2S impacted area ;
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 108 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ All escape routes on the facility leading to muster areas where a H2S hazard exists shall be passable
by personnel wearing SCBA
SELECTION OF GAS DETECTORS
Personal Gas Detectors
Personnel, including temporary workers and visitors entering Red, Amber or Yellow zones shall be provided
with personal H2S detectors which will alarm in the event of exposure to H2S concentrations as mentioned in
Section.
Personal H2S detectors shall have the following minimum specification:
▪ Cannot be switched off either deliberately or accidentally; and
▪ Alarm at the toxic gas warning level in such a way as to be recognizable under all foreseeable
conditions including in high noise areas
▪ A system shall be in place to ensure that personal H2S detectors are properly maintained
▪ Devices shall be tested and calibrated
WORKING IN RED, AMBER AND YELLOW ZONES
Red Zones
All personnel entering a red zone shall have H2S training, competence and certification, carry a personal H2S
monitor and don breathing apparatus and use it at all times.
As a minimum, the following shall be considered:
▪ Access control shall be adhered to as mentioned in Section 7.2.5 of the standard
▪ At all times SCBA set shall be donned and in use and for all prolonged work use airline fed breathing
apparatus shall be ensured
▪ Provide a means of constant communication (i.e. fixed plant telephone or hand held radio) with
someone located outside the Red Zone, in addition to use of the buddy system;
▪ Move any operator rooms/shelter, e.g. those for sour gas compressors, outside the red zones, or
make the operator room a temporary refuge;
▪ Avoid as far as possible any work such as painting, or civil works maintenance until the process plant
is depressurized. This is reasonably practical if the plant is designed for this approach, e.g. has good
segregation distances and is designed for minimum onsite work;
▪ Avoid as far as possible the need for routine work in the Red Zone for example: use transmitters rather
than gauges, and automatic sampling rather than manual sample taking; and
▪ Operating companies shall review whether it is necessary to carry out the tasks in the Red Zone while
the plant is operating. The work activities in the RED Zone shall be minimize to absolute minimum as
far as practicable
Amber Zones
All personnel entering an Amber zone shall have H2S training, competence and certification, shall wear a
personal H2S monitor, and, as a minimum, shall carry an Emergency Escape Breathing Apparatus (EEBA) or
have it at hand’s reach.
For those, involved in performing activities requiring opening of process components which contain, or may
contain H2S (intrusive work/breaking containment) shall have the breathing airline apparatus or SCBA set
donned and in use;
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 109 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
As a minimum the following shall be considered:
▪ Access control shall be adhered to as mentioned in Section 7.2.5 of the standard
▪ Provide a means of constant communication (i.e. fixed plant telephone or hand held radio) with
someone located outside the Amber Zone, in addition to use of the buddy system;
▪ Moving any operator rooms/shelters, e.g. those for sour gas compressors, outside the Amber zone,
or making it a temporary refuge; and
▪ Avoid as far as possible any work such as painting, civil works maintenance until the process plant is
depressurized. This is reasonably practical if the plant is designed for this approach, e.g. has good
segregation distances and is designed for minimum onsite work.
Yellow Zones
Anyone entering a Yellow Zone shall have H2S training, competence and certification, be aware of the location
of strategically placed EEBA sets and shall wear a H2S Monitor.
While the risk of exposure to dangerous concentrations of H2S is much lower when working on equipment in
the yellow zone, there can still be a significant risk of toxic injury and illness during some operations. Opening
of H2S containing equipment shall only be undertaken by persons donning breathing airline apparatus or
SCBA set and a portable H2S detector used for monitoring.
For sampling activity in H2S Zones, SCBA shall be worn and the area be cleared to a safe distance while
opening and isolating and until the equipment is purged and while de-spading and restoring to service. The
area shall be marked off and signed as for Red and Amber Zones during these activities. The size of the
restricted area shall be determined during the Job Safety Assessment (JSA) based on dispersion analysis.
MONITORING EQUIPMENT-MAINTENANCE, TESTING AND CALIBRATION
The equipment used for monitoring the exposure levels of H2S shall be serviced and tested at intervals
recommended by the manufacturer, and possibly more frequently under extreme humidity, temperature, dust
or other adverse environmental operating conditions.
Status calibration shall be checked before every use of portable gas monitors in accordance with the
manufacturer's recommendation. Records of device testing and maintenance shall be kept for verification and
audit purposes.
Bump Testing
Bump testing is the only way to ensure proper sensor and alarm functionality. A bump test is defined as the
process of briefly exposing sensors in a gas detector to an expected concentration of gas that is greater than
the alarm set points. The purpose of the bump test is to check for sensor and alarm functionality. However, it
does not check for accuracy. It is important to note that accuracy is ensured through calibration, which is a
completely different process than bump testing.
The bump test for personal gas detectors shall be done once in a month or according to manufacturer’s
recommendation whichever comes earlier. However, for portable gas monitors used by Authorized Gas Tester
shall be bump tested before every use. Records for the bump testing shall be maintained. Any gas monitor
that fails to respond properly during a functional bump test shall be sent for troubleshooting or further
maintenance as required.
Self-Testing
The personal detectors shall be self-tested each day before the use of the detector. The device will perform a
self-test by operating the buzzer, LED’s, vibrator and LCD. It will also show the configured alarm set points on
the display.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 110 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Calibration
Calibration is a quantitative test utilizing a known traceable concentration of test gas to demonstrate that the
sensor(s) and alarms respond to the gas within manufacturer’s acceptable limits. It is the adjustment of the
baseline and sensitivity of the sensor/electronics circuit, that is, it is based on an exposure to a known gas in
which the sensor/instrument is adjusted to that concentration. Calibrations are required on a periodic basis.
All sensors regardless of the operational principle, will drift over a period of time and require adjustments.
Sensors may also experience drift after an over-exposure to the targeted gas, or after exposure to extreme
environmental conditions, after a severe physical jolt, or if a Function Test produces a less than satisfactory
result. It is important to observe the response times of the sensors, the stability of the sensor signal, and the
recovery times of the sensors during this period.
The calibration interval for each of these sensors from the different makers of the instruments may be different.
The frequency of calibration shall be governed as per the manufacturer’s recommendation as indicated in the
instruction manual for the routine test and re-calibration interval.
TRAINING, COMPETENCE & CERTIFICATION
Training
Training is crucial for enabling people to acquire the necessary skills and knowledge that ensure they are
capable to safely undertake tasks that may expose them to the hazards of H2S. The key to effective training
is to understand the specific job requirements and individual abilities.
H2S training shall therefore be provided to all personnel includes specific potential exposures and include, as
applicable, awareness training, formal classroom instruction, in addition to on the job training and coaching.
Competence
Personnel having potential exposure to H2S, shall be assessed and declared competent against applicable
industry accepted performance standards such as OPITO Basic H2S Training or ANSI/ASSP Z390.1 as
applicable).
Demonstrating required level of Competence for working in hazardous conditions shall involve application of
technical training, demonstration specific skill.
Certification
Documented evidence shall be provided/required in the form of a Certificate from approved training
centre/awarding body to all personnel on successful completion of H2S training.
MINIMUM TRAINING FOR ALL PERSONNEL – LEVEL 1
This applies to all personnel, including temporary workers and visitors, entering a site that has a H2S hazard
shall as a minimum, be trained as per the following:
Basic Awareness
▪ The hazards, characteristics and properties of H2S;
▪ Sources of H2S;
▪ Symptoms of H2S exposure;
▪ Safe exposure limits for H2S;
▪ The meaning of the zone designation and requirements for entering the classified zones;
▪ Use of personal H2S detectors;
▪ Proper use and maintenance of RPE for working in H2S atmospheres, usage instruction and hands-
on practice;

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ The understanding and proper response of warning signs, alarms, both general and from personal
detectors related to H2S;
▪ Recognizing and reporting a H2S leak; and
▪ First aid to victims of H2S exposure.
Site Specific
▪ Regulations pertinent to workers on H2S jobsites;
▪ Proper use of H2S detection methods used at the workplace;
▪ Actions to be taken in the event of an emergency and awareness of emergency muster points;
▪ Wind direction awareness and importance of moving crosswind;
▪ Locations of safe briefing areas;
▪ Escape and Egress routes;
▪ Emergency Response Procedures that have been developed for the site;
▪ Locations and use of safety equipment; and
▪ (requirements for entering/leaving Red Zones Amber or Yellow zones including register in/out
procedure (wherever applicable) and interface with the PTW as per ADNOC Permit to Work Standards
;
Emergency Breathing Apparatus Training
Understand the buddy system and the duties of a buddy (remain alert, raise alarm, keep any rescue lines
clear);
Demonstrate donning of EEBA
▪ Contents of the procedure; and
▪ Proper use and donning of EEBA for working in H2S atmosphere.
▪ Must be able to connect and disconnect from a cascade system (as applicable)
LEVEL 2 TRAINING
This applies to all personnel entering to H2S Zones (Red). In addition to Section 7.5.2 of the standard, as a
minimum the following topics shall also be covered:
▪ Requirements for and reasons behind facial hair restrictions (Refer ADNOC Personal Protective
Equipment Standards;
▪ Operational controls applicable for Red Zones (refer Section 7.4 of the standard);
▪ Consequences of not adhering to procedures;
▪ Use of PPE required by operational controls, which must include both self-contained breathing
apparatus, emergency escape mask and air-line fed breathing apparatus; and
▪ Use of PPE in an emergency;
Use of Breathing Apparatus (SCBA/Breathing Airline) in H2S Zones (Red) Before issue of a H2S training
Certificate, personnel attending a H2S training course shall:
▪ Demonstrate understanding of the course topics;
▪ Demonstrate the ability to correctly don SCBA in 45 seconds
▪ Demonstrate correct use of breathing apparatus; and
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ Demonstrate correct operation and use of airline fed breathing apparatus
Personnel who will be working with breathing apparatus (Tight Fit) shall be ’fit tested’ for the specific full face
mask which will be used by them. They shall also demonstrate understanding & competency in:
▪ Understanding of breathing airline supplies;
▪ Understanding the importance of keeping lines free and untangled;
▪ Understanding the importance of having a person standing by to assist in case of difficulties;
▪ The ability to check connections and air supply;
▪ The ability to change over connections safely;
▪ Emergency air supply in case of breathing airline failure; and
▪ The procedure for escape
6.45 MARINE OPERATIONS
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-OS-ST24 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall conduct Marine operations/activities as per the relevant laws and regulations, ADNOC HSE
Standards and ADNOC Group requirements.
Contractors shall follow ADNOC Group Marine Safety Management system/procedures.
Contractors must obtain permits and approvals where relevant e.g. vehicle access in jetty, Petroleum Port
authority requirements, Marine permit, diving permits etc.
Where transfer of personnel is performed by speedboat, vessels shall be equipped with means approved by
the ADNOC Group allowing a safe transfer.
Whatever is the means of transfer and during work overboard, all personnel shall wear a life jacket approved
by IMO SOLAS (with neck protection) and training shall be provided to all concerned personnel including
visitors.
The inflatable life jacket with cartridge must be inspected and certified by third party.
Diving activities and positioning shall be performed as per ADNOC Group procedures/ guidelines and in line
with ADNOC HSE Standards.
Maintain records of certification of personnel and equipment.
Pre mobilization inspection of vessels and barges shall be conducted by the ADNOC Group.
Contractors shall comply with Marine HSE Policy
MARINE HSE POLICY
The Marine HSE policy shall incorporate:
▪ The commitment and expectations of 100% HSE for marine activities;
▪ The commitment to strive for continuous improvement in marine HSE performance;
▪ The importance of marine HSE objectives in comparison with other business and HSE objectives;
▪ The promotion of openness and participation of all individuals in improving HSE performance;
- The company organization and line management responsible for marine HSE
- The commitment to comply UAE Federal Laws and other relevant regulations, including this
Standards;
- Challenging the requirement for marine operations and consideration of alternatives that may
have a lower exposure to HSE risks, and/or which use less fuel

ADNOC Classification: Internal
- Available in Arabic and English;
- Given to each employee engaged in marine operations by their supervisor and the implications of
non-compliance explained in practical terms;
- Displayed in prominent locations, such as notice boards, offices and meeting rooms;
- Included in marine operations manuals;
- Discussed and explained during training courses
MARINE CREW REQUIREMENTS
ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA FOR MARINE CONTRACTORS
Prior to the start of any contract, ADNOC Group shall ensure that the Contractor is required to have as a
minimum an experienced and qualified Port Captain (or Marine Superintendent), Port Engineer (or Engineer
Superintendent) and HSE Manager/Engineer/Officer within its UAE shore management support structure.
Additional support may be required depending on the number of vessels and the nature of the work conducted
by the contractor.
The minimum certification requirement for these Contractors shall be in line to the requirements of Standards
of Training, Certification and Watch keeping for Seafarers (STCW) [Ref. 1] as applicable. It establishes basic
requirements on training, certification and watch-keeping on an internationally consistent basis.
ADNOC Group shall ensure that all personnel associated to marine operations shall undergo the requirements
and be certified as mentioned in STCW, as applicable.
The CVs of Contractor's senior marine personnel shall be reviewed and approved before engagement of any
vessel on contract under ADNOC Group control, and in accordance with the relevant "Marine Crew
Familiarization Policies & Procedures".
COMPETENCE & CERTIFICATION SCREENING
General Competence Requirements
A minimum competence/training requirement for vessel crew and all personnel who have HSE critical
responsibilities shall be established.
For key marine positions, a program of competence assessment shall be demonstrated by the Contractors
and shall be subject to verification by the ADNOC Group.
Competence/training requirements shall apply to both ADNOC Employees and Contractor's.
Marine crew competence shall as a minimum be in accordance with the requirements stated in STCW or
equivalent.
Persons undergoing training shall not be considered as part of the vessel’s complement for the purpose of
minimum safe manning levels.
Contractor Competence Requirements
Marine contractors shall have an experienced and qualified Port Captain (or Marine Superintendent), Port
Engineer (or Engineer Superintendent) and HSE Manager/Engineer/Officer within their shore management
structure.
The qualifications and minimum competence requirements for these persons shall be detailed within the
Contractor Company’s HSE Management System. In turn, these persons shall make sure that only
appropriately qualified and experienced marine personnel are appointed to vessels under their responsibility.
The Curriculum Vitae of the contractor's senior marine personnel shall be reviewed and approved by ADNOC
Group before engagement of any vessel on contract.
Safety training of Contractor's personnel shall only be recognized if conducted by organizations or individuals
that have been approved by ADNOC Group. Contractors shall be required to keep records to demonstrate
that such safety training has been carried out as required.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 114 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
New marine crew members shall attend ADNOC Group induction sessions in order to gain familiarity with, and
be made fully aware of, established ADNOC Group safety procedures and regulations.
Contractors shall ensure availability of appropriately number of trained senior crew members on its vessels,
which shall include as a minimum Master, Chief Engineer, and Chief Mate.
ADNOC Group shall verify compliance with these requirements (including physical examination of certificates)
during the pre-acceptance vessel inspection and also during the interim audits.
WORK ROTATION SCHEDULES
All crew members and personnel associated with marine activities (i.e. the Master, Chief Engineer, Chief Mate,
personnel on marine vessels, other officers etc.) shall have work a rotation period as applicable to the ADNOC
Group Companies operations.
DIVING CONTRACTOR
On any diving project/operation, there needs to be one company in overall control of the diving operations.
This will normally be the company who employs the divers. If there is more than one company employing
divers then there shall be a written agreement as to which of these companies is in overall control. The
company in control is called the Diving Contractor.
The name of the Diving Contractor shall be clearly displayed. All personnel, clients and others involved in the
diving operation shall be aware who the Diving Contractor is.
The Diving Contractor shall provide a defined management structure in writing. This shall include
arrangements for a clear hand over of supervisory responsibilities at appropriate stages in the operation, again
recorded in writing.
The Diving Contractor’s responsibilities shall include provisions to ensure that:
▪ Risk assessments have been carried out both onshore and at site.
▪ The place from which operations are to be carried out is suitable and safe.
▪ There are sufficient personnel of the required competency in the diving team.
▪ The personnel are qualified and competent. Divers shall be medically qualified for using breathing
apparatus/equipment for diving and certified by accredited institute for diving operation.
▪ The plant and equipment is correctly certified and properly maintained.
▪ Before any diving is carried out there should be an approved Diving Project Plan in existence. The
approved Diving Project Plan should consist of approved project specific documents. [IMCA D 014;
Ref. 40, Section 7.1 “Diving Project Plan”.]
▪ Suitable site-specific safety and familiarisation training is provided to all members of the dive and
support teams.
▪ Adequate arrangements exist for first aid and medical treatment of personnel.
▪ Emergency response and contingency plan to be updated and in place.
▪ Contractors should have a Diving Management System (DMS) in place and implemented.
▪ Appropriate insurance policies which shall include third party liability and appropriate insurance cover
all divers.
▪ There is a clear reporting and responsibility structure laid out in writing.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 115 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ Diving Supervisors are appointed in writing and the extent of their control is clearly documented.
DIVING SUPERVISOR
The training and competence requirements for a Diving supervisor are detailed in IMCA C 003 Rev 3.1,
February 2017: “Guidance on Competence Assurance and Assessment”.
Diving Supervisors are responsible for the operation that they have been appointed to supervise, and they
shall only hand over control to another supervisor appointed in writing by the Diving Contractor. Such a
handover shall be entered in the relevant operations logbook.
Diving Supervisors can only supervise as much of a diving operation as they can personally control both during
routine operations and if an emergency should occur.
The Diving Supervisor with overall responsibility for the operation is the only person who can order the start
of a dive, subject to appropriate work permits etc. Other relevant parties, such as the relevant Area Authority
(as defined in the Work Management System) can, however, tell the Diving Supervisor to terminate a dive for
safety or operational reasons.
There will be times, for example, during operations from a vessel using dynamic positioning (DP) techniques
that the Diving Supervisor will need to liaise closely with other personnel, such as the vessel Master or the DP
operator. In such circumstances, the Diving Supervisor shall recognize that the vessel’s Master has
responsibility for the overall safety of the vessel and its occupants.
The Diving Supervisor is entitled to give direct orders in relation to health and safety to any person taking part
in, or who has any influence over, the diving operation. These orders take precedence over any company
hierarchy. These orders could include instructing unnecessary personnel to leave a control area, instructing
personnel to operate equipment, etc. This authority does not extend to the duties of the Master of the vessel.
To ensure that the diving operation is carried out safely, Diving Supervisors shall consider a number
of points regarding the workforce including that:
They are competent to carry out such work, and that they understand their own areas and levels of
responsibility and who is responsible for any other relevant areas. Such responsibilities shall be contained in
the relevant documentation. They shall be in possession of a letter from the Diving Contractor appointing
them as a Diving Supervisor.
They are competent to carry out the work required of them. This includes that personnel are fit and in
possession of a valid medical certificate of fitness.
Equipment they propose to use for any particular operation is adequate, safe, properly certified and maintained.
They can do this by confirming that the equipment meets the requirements set down in this Standards.
Equipment is adequately checked by themselves or another competent person prior to its use. Such checks
shall be documented, for example, on a pre-prepared checklist, and recorded in the diving operations log for
the project.
When the operation uses, or plans to use, complex or potentially hazardous equipment, they shall ensure that
the possible hazards have been evaluated and are fully understood by all relevant parties and that, if required,
training is given. This shall be carried out as part of the risk assessment during the planning of the operation
and shall be documented. If the situation changes, further risk assessment shall be considered. Diving
Supervisors shall meet their responsibilities by ensuring the documentation exists and following any guidance
contained in the documentation, e.g. manufacturer’s instructions.
The operation they are being asked to supervise complies with the requirements of this Standards. Detailed
advice on how they can ensure this is given in various sections of this Standards.
All involved parties are aware that a diving operation is going to start or continue. Obtain any necessary
permission before starting or continuing the operation, normally via a Permit to Work (PTW) system.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 116 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Have clear, audible and visual communications with any personnel under their supervision, with either direct
vision or using assisting aids. For example, a Diving Supervisor will be able to control the raising and lowering
of a diving bell adequately if there is a direct audio link with the winch operator, even though the winch may
be physically located where the supervisor cannot see it nor have ready access to it.
The Diving Supervisor also needs to have clear communication with other personnel at the diving location,
such as: marine crew; DP operator; crane operator; and ROV personnel (Refer to IMCA D 023, D 024, D 046
/ M 205, and D 054 / R 020 [Ref. 46, 47, 58, and 64 respectively).
During saturation or bell operations, Diving Supervisors shall be able to see the divers inside the bell or
compression chamber. This will normally be achieved on the surface by means of direct viewing through the
view ports but when the bell is under water this shall be by means of an internal camera.
Direct communications with any diver in the water at all times, even if another person needs to talk to, or listen
to, the diver [IMCA D 022].
Comprehensive Risk Assessment (e.g. JSA) shall be carried out prior to commencement of the work. A formal
process for Risk Assessment is required to be followed by the Diving Contractor, in compliance with the
ADNOC HSE Standards “HSE Risk Management System” HSE-RM-ST01.
Refer further to IMCA C003 for the relevant Competence and Assurance requirements for a Diving Supervisor.
6.46 AIR TRANSPORTATION
Contractors shall refer to ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE-OS-ST26 for details and necessary compliance.
Minimum requirements:
Helicopter transportation is a common mode of travel to and from offshore work sites as well as to and from
remote onshore work sites. The Contractor shall follow Civil Aviation Advisory Publication (CAAP)
requirements.
Utilize the ADNOC Group approved Flights and Helicopters only.
When helicopter transportation may potentially occur over water, personnel shall pass certified Helicopter
Underwater Escape Training (HUET) and Sea survival training prior to transportation and refresher training
shall be attended by those personnel as per the frequency stipulated by the ADNOC Group.
Personnel shall participate in a safety instruction video and briefing prior to each transport via helicopter which
shall include: identification of hazards and associated safety controls related to inflight helicopter
transportation, personnel loading/unloading and cargo loading/unloading, identification of protective
equipment (e.g. seat belts, noise protection, personal flotation devices) during in-flight helicopter
transportation, emergency procedures.
All passengers and cargo to be weighed and manifested.
The safe /prohibited work practices associated with inflight helicopter transportation, personnel
loading/unloading and cargo loading/unloading shall be followed as per the ADNOC HSE Standards and
ADNOC Group guidelines.
Simultaneous operations between helicopter/flight operations and cranes shall be adequately addressed as
per the ADNOC Group requirements where applicable.
Winching operations shall be conducted as per ADNOC Group approved procedures.
Risks from air transport operations shall be assessed and control measures implemented to ensure a level
that is As Low as Reasonably Practicable (ALARP).
Contractors shall take into account the possibility of simultaneous operations when conducting risk
assessments for any operation, which involves air transport operations in line with ADNOC HSE Standards
on Work Management Systems.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 117 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Portable Electronic Devices
No person shall use a portable electronic device on board an aircraft that can adversely affect the performance
of the aircraft. ADNOC Group authorized, as PAO shall ensure that appropriate instructions are in place for
the carriage and use of portable electronic devices on board.
Smoking and Alcohol Policy
Smoking is prohibited at all times in any type of aircraft, on a helicopter landing area or within the boundary of
aerodrome. Smoking shall be controlled to prevent unnecessary risks for air transport operations.
Personnel under the influence of alcohol or drugs shall not be allowed to board any aircraft operating, for, or
on behalf of the ADNOC Group.
All personnel shall comply with ADNOC smoking and alcohol policies.
Carrying Freight with Passenger
The carriage of freight in the cabin with the passengers is permitted within certain defined rules. Urgent freight,
including mail bags, shall normally be loaded in the freight/baggage compartment. In exceptional
circumstances only, it may be carried in the cabin with passengers, but strictly subject to the following
conditions:
▪ It shall not obstruct main emergency exits or access routes available to the passengers
▪ It shall not obstruct secondary exit windows adjacent to occupied seats
▪ It shall be securely tied down to aircraft strong points or with a tensioned net acceptable to the aircraft
commander
▪ If seats are folded up, care shall be taken to ensure that they are stowed in such a way that they do
not present a hazard to any passenger in an emergency.
Carrying Dangerous Goods by Air
Any substance and/article shall not be carried for air transport under any circumstances that:
▪ is liable to explode;
▪ can dangerously react;
▪ can produce a flame or dangerous evolution of heat or dangerous omission of toxic, corrosive or
flammable gases or vapours under conditions normally encountered in transport
▪ is prohibited by the GCAA.
Prohibited or Restricted Articles
In addition to restrictions on dangerous goods, other items/articles may be prohibited or restricted by the GCAA
and the UAE Ministry of Interior. These items include firearms, ammunition, matches, pesticides, weapons
etc. Knives which are tools of trade (e.g. chefs and divers) shall be declared at check-in.
The carriage of cameras (including those in smart phones, laptops, tablets) and binoculars shall be restricted
in accordance with oil field security requirements, unless specific permission has been given prior to personnel
checking in for a flight.
The list of items usually prohibited by the GCAA and/or UAE Ministry of Interior for carriage on aircraft or to
offshore installations shall be referred to. This shall be displayed at dispatch counters for passengers to view
and read at all locations in Abu Dhabi such as all ADNOC Group air transport check in desks and all UAE
based non-ADNOC Group aerodromes/helicopter landing areas where flights to and from ADNOC areas
originate.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Night Flying
Night flying operations shall not be allowed in ADNOC unless necessary approval / dispensations have been
granted by relevant local authorities and for emergency purposes.
6.47 SITE RESTORATION/DEMOBILIZATION
Minimum requirements
Contractors shall comply with ADNOC Group demobilization plan, restore the site through proper
housekeeping, and ensure all the HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements are implemented during this phase
in line with contractual requirements.
Upon Completion of the Work or Services, contractors shall restore the site satisfactorily, including any areas
of the ADNOC Group process/restricted areas and any other premises thereon, used to perform the work or
services by the contractor.
Contractors shall ensure that all surplus materials, buildings, structures, equipment, etc. have been cleared
from sites and compounds allocated for the Contractor on the ADNOC Group controlled areas whether on
onshore, Island, Offshore or Abu Dhabi areas to the satisfaction of ADNOC Group Contract Administrator.
Contractors shall not be allowed to demobilize HSE critical personnel, equipment, plant, etc. prior to obtaining
written permission from the ADNOC Group contract administrator / representative.
