APPENDIX 1: CONTRACTORS’ MEDICAL REQUIREMENTS
GENERAL
According to the UAE Labour Law and other relevant laws of UAE, ADNOC HSE Standards , ADNOC Group
Medical Fitness Guidelines (Latest version), any person applying for a job in the ADNOC Group or its
Contractors and their Sub-contractors, shall undergo a medical examination and be found fit for such
employment. ADNOC / Group Companies require all Contractors and their Sub-contractors engaged in
ADNOC/ Group business to comply with this requirement with respect to all their employees.
1. Contractors shall ensure that all personnel engaged in the work are medically fit for the job to be
undertaken and provide results/fitness certificate to ADNOC Group. Medical examination reports/fitness
certificates to be submitted to site administration for records tracking.
2. Every Contractor shall appoint or subscribe for occupational health services (pre-employment medical
assessment, periodic medical assessment, health surveillance, return-to-work fitness assessment), by a
medical facility licensed by UAE Government Health Authorities. ADNOC provides a list of the preferred
healthcare facilities that are meeting the ADNOC Group Medical Fitness Requirements. Contractor may
preferably avail Medical Fitness Certificate (MFC) from these approved facilities.
3. For oversees medical examinations, contractor may utilize UAE accredited medical facilities that are
affiliate / branches in other countries, where required with prior approval from ADNOC. However, such
contractors employees shall be required to undergo same tests within 6-12 month from mobilization.
4. Contractors and their Sub-contractors shall ensure at all times that, a current valid Medical Fitness
Certificate (MFC) is maintained for each of its personnel working at or visiting any of ADNOC Group’s
Work Locations.
5. Contractors and their Sub-contractors shall be required to submit an up-to-date status of medical fitness
of all the employees with copies of their “valid” medical fitness certificates (MFC). All documents referred
to herein shall be made available for inspection and verification by ADNOC / Group Companies prior to
the mobilization of Contractor’s personnel to a Work Location. ADNOC and ADNOC Group Companies
reserves the right to inspect or audit such documents at any time to ensure the compliance with the
requirements stated herein.
6. The assessing physician may recommend increasing the frequency of periodic assessments if deemed
clinically necessary, such as for those persons with chronic, but not disqualifying conditions. In some
circumstances specific testing may be required, e.g. visual acuity, auditory acuity etc. as may be
prescribed by the physician. These periodic assessment / surveillance requirements shall be monitored
by the Contractor.
If the injury or illness of an employee necessitates giving him time off from work (more than 14 days),
he/she shall be re-examined and certified fit before he can resume duty.
7. Where an employee has been evacuated from a work location for medical reasons, he shall be thoroughly
examined and an up-to-date MFC must be issued prior to his return to duty (Return to Work Fitness).
APPLICATION
The fitness criteria set out in this document are the minimum requirements for occupational health
assessments/evaluation in ADNOC Group and should not be considered as all inclusive. Additional criteria
may be included based on risk assessment as determined by the nature of the job and its location.
The fitness criteria shall also be applicable to assessments, which occur at different stages of employment
within the ADNOC Group. This includes pre-employment/pre-placement, pre-enrolment; specific jobs
examinations, periodic medical examination, referrals for an occupational health assessment for continuing
fitness for work,
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 120 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Where fitness criteria are not described (for a specific job type) in these guidelines, comparable industry
standards and/or international best practice for equivalent jobs can be referred to as criteria for assessment.
Decision on fitness for work in such cases will be reviewed and/or approved by ADNOC Group Medical
Committee (AGMC).
CHANGE OF JOB MEDICAL EXAMINATION
Change of job medical examinations shall be carried out at all times where the employee is assigned to a new
job and/or to a new work environment. In this regard the change of job medical examination has special
relevance where the new job and work environment can pose significant risk to health and safety of the
employee and others in the workplace.
The fitness for work shall be assessed against the general fitness criteria described in this guideline and where
there are specific requirements for the particular job, against the additional requirements for that post.
REMOTE AREA & OFFSHORE VISIT MEDICAL ASSESSMENT
If a Contractor has Department of Health (DOH) or relevant government authorities approved medical
facility/Clinic at site, medical screening procedure may be conducted at that medical facility/ Clinic.
Contractors and non-company visitors requiring remote area/offshore visits should have a valid fitness to work
certificate. They shall hence undergo additional tests depending on the location or the purpose of the visit.
Simple medical screening procedure (e.g. physical examination, vital signs, visual acuity, history) may be
adopted for individuals undertaking short-duration visits (<24hrs) to remote and offshore locations (Furnace
et al, 2008).
For those undertaking a remote/offshore area visit longer than 24 hours, complete medical examination shall
be conducted in accordance with this guideline.
ALCOHOL AND DRUG SCREENING
Where indicated, alcohol and drug screening shall be conducted in accordance with UAE Law and applicable
Department of Health (Previously Health Authority of Abu Dhabi (HAAD) or location specific equivalent Health
authority standards for screening and analytical detection methodologies for alcohol and drugs in biological
samples (HAAD, 2012).
Pre-employment / periodic Drug and Alcohol screening shall be completed on designated candidates (defined
as a direct hire (periodic), new hire, or re-hire) based on the technical work condition or job type. It may also
be performed on personnel performing safety critical jobs including fire fighter, Divers, seafarers, radiation
workers, emergency responders etc. based on the risk assessment and/or at the discretion of the concerned
ADNOC Group to which the employee is affiliated. All such requests should be forwarded to Group Medical
Division well in advance towards ensuring that adequate controls are in place for performing Drug and Alcohol
screening in accordance with UAE legislations, UAE Labor Law, applicable Department of Health (DOH)
standards and ADNOC policies and procedures.
In general applicable provisions of Oil and Gas Contractor Drug and Alcohol Testing guidelines
(2016) - International Association of Oil & Gas Producers shall be applied for alcohol and Drug
Screening.
Group Medical Division shall reserve the right to release the results of said tests to the concerned Company
or to any UAE governmental entity as per the applicable law or if involved in a legal proceeding / investigation
connected with the test.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 121 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH SURVEILLANCE AND BIOLOGICAL MONITORING
Where required and as identified under OHRA by the ADNOC Group, Contractors shall implement
recommendations from OHRA and perform Occupational Health Risk Assessment (OHRA) during the life
cycle of the contract.
Risk related health surveillance is conducted in conjunction with the Occupational Health Risk Assessment
(OHRA) and implemented for similar exposure groups (SEG) with extreme, high and medium occupational
health risks, where such risks cannot be reduced at source. OHRA is the responsibility of ADNOC Group
and contractors respectively.
The frequency and content of occupational health surveillance and biological monitoring will depend on the
risk of exposure, the extent of control of hazards, compliance with preventive measures, and on the
regulatory and legal requirements.
The procedures for occupational health risk assessment, occupational health surveillance and biological
monitoring shall be in accordance with applicable ADNOC HSE Standards on occupational health
management.
UAE Labour Law also requires six (6) monthly medical examinations for employees who are at risk of exposure
to a list of prescribed agents.
OCCUPATIONAL ILLNESS INVESTIGATION & REPORTING
Contractors shall collate their occupational Illness statistics and provide appropriate reports to ADNOC Group
Cases of suspected or confirmed occupational illnesses should be investigated in collaboration with health,
environmental, safety and occupational hygiene specialists. Any relevant preventive measures should be
considered and implemented as appropriate.
The investigations shall be carried out in accordance with applicable ADNOC HSE Standards.
Suspected cases of occupational illness shall be reported to a nominated competent person (with respective
company) who will consider each case and coordinate appropriate follow-up. The company shall collate their
occupational health statistics and provide appropriate reports to ADNOC.
INFECTIOUS DISEASES & IMMUNIZATION
The Department of Health (previously Health Authority of Abu Dhabi (HAAD) or location specific equivalent
Health authority mandates immunization for workers in specific jobs (e.g. food handlers, barbers, healthcare
workers, sewage workers). Where such jobs are relevant for ADNOC Group Contractors or Sub-contractors
shall ensure implementation of the same.
CONTRACTOR shall implement requirements of latest versions of Business Continuity Readiness Guidelines
for UAE Organizations [AE/SCNS/NCEMA 7002:2020 Version II March 2020] including pandemic specific
directives or standing instructions from relevant UAE government authorities, ADNOC pandemic specific
guidelines and protocols such as COVID-19, as applicable, to reduce the risks and minimize the impact on
people and operations
Minor infectious disease without the risk of spread is likely to be acceptable for remote area and offshore
work. However, all individuals should be restricted from remote and offshore work during the active stages of
an infectious disease if there is significant risk of spread within the working community.
Individuals suffering from chronic communicable disease should be assessed to determine:
a. The risk of transmission to others;
b. Any effects of the condition which may adversely affect the individual’s ability to perform their duties
or effectively participate in emergency evacuation; and
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 122 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
c. The requirements of long-term therapy and side effects of such therapy.
Catering staff should undergo special examination to exclude acute or chronic disease involving
gastrointestinal tract, chest, ear, nose, throat and skin due to the risk of food-borne spread of the disease.
Routine screening for tuberculosis during periodic medical examination is not required unless indicated.
HEALTH PROMOTION
Contractors shall conduct workplace health promotion campaigns to educate their personnel on different
health hazards and control measures. Health Promotion should include awareness about Occupational Health
Hazards, Risk and Prevention as well as promotion of Healthy Lifestyle and Mental Health.
MEDICAL RECORDS AND CONFIDENTIALITY OF INFORMATION
Medical records shall be kept confidential and access to such records should be given to authorize persons
only. Medical records / health information retention and disposal (regardless of the media – paper, electronics,
and films) shall be properly maintained as required for patient care purposes in compliance with ADNOC HSE
Standards and applicable local & federal regulatory requirements.
HEALTH INSURANCE
d. Provision of health insurance shall be in compliance with applicable UAE Laws and Regulations
e. Contractors shall provide valid health insurance to their employees in line with UAE Federal Law
ensuring improved access and adequate coverage to medical services within the acceptable vicinity
of work locations.
f. Contractors shall provide to their employees at contractors’ own expense medical treatment in line
with UAE Labour Law
NON-ACCIDENTAL DEATHS
a. Contractors shall inform ADNOC Group immediately of a non-accidental death of their employee in
line with ADNOC incident notification, reporting and investigation standards
b. Contractors will be expected to initiate, participate and/ or contribute to investigation in the event of
such death
c. Contractors shall ensure implementation of the recommendations following the investigation
ACCIDENTAL DEATHS
a) Contractors shall inform ADNOC Group immediately of an accidental death of their employee as per
ADNOC incident notification, reporting and investigation standards
b) Contractors will be expected to initiate, participate and/ or contribute to any investigation in the event
of such death
c) Contractors shall ensure implementation of the recommendations following the investigation
MEDICAL EXAMINATIONS
When Contractors and their Sub-contractors employees have been declared medically fit to work with any one
of the ADNOC Group Companies, engaged again for the same nature of work (which should be satisfactorily
demonstrated through defined job description) they may not repeat medical assessment while executing works
with another Group Company, subject to the validity of initial test.
Irrespective of the above, any employee may be required to undergo complete/additional medical assessment
as required based on the identified risk at work place.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 123 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
When an employee is declared medically fit for onshore works and the same employee is required to execute
similar activity offshore, complete medical assessment shall be conducted, based on the identified risks.
Pre-Employment Medical Examination
Pre-employment medical examinations shall be conducted for all recruits to ensure that they are fit for the job
and tasks to which they will be assigned. The main objective is to ensure that the individual is fit to perform
job tasks safely and effectively (without risk to their own or others’ health and safety) and that they do not have
clinical conditions that can be aggravated by their work.
Fitness for work should be judged against the general fitness criteria as per ADNOC Group requirements and
against additional requirements (if any) for that particular job. This principle shall also apply to other medical
assessments outlined in the subsequent (following) sections.
Periodic Medical Examination
Periodic medical examinations shall be carried out for employees to ensure they remain fit for their job and
that their health has not been adversely affected by their work. It also enables recommendations to be made
to accommodate any health problems as and when applicable.
Unless otherwise required for specific job types (e.g. diver, firefighter, seafarer, radiation worker, food
handler, etc.) or by UAE Law for specific occupational exposures and/or following occupational health
assessment, the minimum frequency of periodic medical examinations shall be as below:
Category Description
Town-based workers under age 40 Every 3 years
Town-based workers age 40-49 Every 2 years
Town-based workers age 50 and above Annually
Remote and offshore workers under age 49 Every 2 years
Remote and offshore workers age 50 and above Annually
Firefighter/ Emergency response team/Diver/Food Handler/or safety
Critical jobs/or upon discretional advice of occupational medicine Annually
p hysician
Seafarers/Drivers/Crane & Forklift Operators / other heavy duty Under age 49: every 2 years
vehicle operators Age 50 and above: Annually
Other categories (e.g. radiation worker) As prescribed by legislation
Medical Examination Contents
Medical examination shall be carried out by the attending physician. A female physician should examine
female candidates / employees. Where a female physician is not available, examination can be performed
by a male physician in the presence of a female chaperone e.g. nurse. Written consent of the
candidate/employee should be obtained prior to physical examination.
Physical examination results should be documented in the appropriate medical exanimation form. Minimum
standards tests for specific job types are provided in Appendix-B of ADNOC Medical Fitness Guidelines (AHQ-
HCA-GSA-GID-003-R00-19). Additional tests (e.g. stress-ECG, spirometry, etc.) may be performed as
determined by the examining physician based on the clinical indications.
Standards Documentation
Contractors and their Sub-contractors employees shall present a valid identification document (UAE ID,
Passport) prior to commencing medical examination.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 124 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Candidates/employees should present relevant referral letter from the concerned company indicating the
reason for medical examination (e.g. Pre-Employment Medical Examination, Periodic Medical Examination)
prior to attending medical examination.
The following documents/forms should be completed and included as a minimum in the pre-
employment medical examination:
a) Copy of Contractor referral letter
b) Health Questionnaire (Arabic or English)
c) Candidate/employee consent form
d) Medical Examination Form
e) Chest X-Ray report
f) Urine test report
g) Blood test reports
h) Anti-HIV, Anti-HCV and HBsAg serology test report
i) Electrocardiogram
j) Spirometry report
k) Audiogram
l) Questionnaire for Food Handlers (as applicable)
m) Radiation Worker Health Surveillance Form (as applicable)
n) Fitness to Wear Respiratory Protection Evaluation Form (as applicable)
Additional reports may be included where supplementary tests are required for specific job types (driver, diver,
food handler, etc.), or as indicated by the assessing physician (e.g. CT, MRI, Echo-CG, stress ECG). Where
specialist examination and opinion is required, a relevant assessment report should be included in the medical
examination record.
Medical History
Candidate/employee occupational, medical and family history shall be recorded using the standards health
questionnaire. The completed questionnaire should be signed by the candidate/employee and examining
doctor.
The medical history shall focus on medical, surgical, obstetric and psychiatric conditions; long-term and
chronic ill health conditions; medication (current and past); allergies (Medication, food, chemicals and
environmental agents); childhood illnesses; immunization, health screening and lifestyle issues.
The following information should be recorded as a minimum:
a) Eye and vision problems
b) Ear nose and throat disorders
c) Respiratory diseases
d) Cardiovascular disorders
e) Gastrointestinal tract disorders
f) Hernias
g) Genitourinary tract disorders
h) Obstetric and gynaecological disorders (where applicable)
i) Musculoskeletal disorders
j) Central and peripheral neural system disorders
k) Mental health / psychiatric disorders
l) Blood disorders
m) Endocrine system disorders
n) Infections (bacterial, viral, tropical, parasitic, zoonosis)
o) Sexually transmitted diseases
p) Communicable diseases
q) Regular and maintenance medications
r) Allergies to medications, food, chemical and environmental agents
s) Malignancies
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 125 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
t) Hospitalizations
u) Past surgeries and major operations
v) Smoking and alcohol habits
w) Childhood illnesses
x) Immunization
y) Subject to health screening, if any.
The assessing physician shall discuss all positive answers in the questionnaire with the examinee and record
the results. In all cases where information available from the history/examination is insufficient to make an
assessment, the assessing physician shall request further relevant medical information from the employee’s
personal physician.
Occupational History
The purpose of occupational history taking is to:
a) Identify occupational risk factors for illness;
b) Understand job demands;
c) Advise on fitness for work; and
d) Advise on accommodation, adjustment, rehabilitation and redeployment.
The following information should be recorded as a minimum requirement:
a) Complete employment history (dates, job types, organization & industry)
b) History of previous occupational illnesses and injuries
c) History of previous sickness absence due to occupational illness and/or injury
d) Present acute/chronic occupational illness
e) History of occupational exposure to hazardous agents (physical, chemical, biological, ergonomics
and psychosocial); and
f) History of industrial / occupational disability
Family History
The family history outlines the age and health, or age and cause of death, of each immediate relative, including
parents, grandparents, siblings, children, and grandchildren. The review of the family history includes the
presence or absence in the family of hypertension, coronary artery disease, hyper/dyslipidaemia, stroke,
diabetes, thyroid or renal disease, cancer, arthritis, tuberculosis, asthma or lung disease, headache, seizure
disorder, mental illness, suicide, alcohol or drug addiction, allergies, familial and genetic diseases as well as
symptoms reported by the candidate/employee.
The family history should include following as minimum:
a) Heart disease or hypertension
b) Allergies
c) Anemia or inherited haemoglobinopathies
d) Asthma
e) Tuberculosis
f) Stroke
g) Epilepsy
h) Kidney disease
i) Diabetes
j) Cancer
k) Mental disorders
l) Hepatitis
The candidate/employee shall sign and acknowledge the information provided on occupational, medical and
family history, as indicating a true and correct record and should understand the significance of doing so.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 126 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
Positive answers or indications in the history should be discussed with the candidate/employee to understand
the extent of the problem, obtain relevant medical reports, if available, and develop an assessment plan
including additional tests and/or obtain specialist consultation, when necessary.
Consent
The Contractors employee shall sign the consent form indicating his/her agreement, complete understanding
and acceptance of medical examination and its mandatory components, (physical assessment, instrumental
and laboratory testing), and additional testing and consultant examination, as necessary.
The signed consent form should be included in the medical examination record.
Where a candidate/employee refuses to sign the consent form, the case should be referred to the Company
Human Resources for further actions as per the company policy. At this stage, the medical examination should
stop.
Job Description
The job description should be provided by the referring company for physician’s reference and consideration
when assessing the functional capacity of the candidate/employee. The job description can help estimate the
risk to health in case of a medical condition requiring treatment and concerns over safe work performance.
Where additional information on workplace health hazards and associated risks is deemed necessary, the
company should provide an occupational health risk assessment report for the candidate's/employee's job
type.
Food Handlers
Food handlers shall undergo a medical examination prior to undertaking work involving the preparation,
cooking, serving, and storage of food. They should have good personal hygiene, be free from relevant
infections, and have a valid food handler certificate issued by Abu Dhabi Agriculture and Food Safety Authority
(ADAFSA)(Previously ADFCA)
Food handlers, who are returning to work from home country leave, should undergo medical
assessment prior to resumption of duties
The examining physician should pay particular attention to presence of fever, jaundice, skin (hair,
nails, hands, arms and face), and respiratory and gastrointestinal system disorders.
Periodicity of Medical Examination
Medical examination of food handlers shall be conducted at pre-employment stage and thereafter at least
annually. Where medical examinations are carried out by a third party healthcare provider, they should adhere
to the recommendations in this guideline.
Medical examination
The minimum medical examination contents for food handlers are as shown in table of this document.
Additional tests can be performed as determined by the examining physician based on the clinical indications.
Fitness Criteria
Food handlers with any of the following conditions shall be excluded from work in order to undergo further
medical evaluation and treatment. They should not be considered fit for return to food handling duties until
deemed non-infectious. Examples of relevant infections include:
a. Salmonella typhi, Salmonella paratyphi, Shigella sp
b. Hepatitis A and E
c. Intestinal parasites (Entamoeba histolytica, Hookworms, Giardia)
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 127 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
d. Active Pulmonary Tuberculosis
e. Skin infections (e.g. Staph. aureus)
Food handlers should be encouraged to report the following symptoms for further medical evaluation and
treatment:
a. Respiratory tract: fever, cough, colds, sore throat
b. Gastrointestinal tract: fever, vomiting, diarrhoea, jaundice; and
c. Skin: boils, infected wound, infected skin rash, especially on hands or arms
All cases of gastro-enteritis or enteritis which may be infective shall be excluded from work until 48 hours after
the cessation of diarrhoea and vomiting. They may then return to work as long as they are clinically well with
normal well-formed stools (48 hour rule). For specific gastrointestinal infections, the opinion of an infectious
disease specialist should be obtained regarding suitability to return to work.
All food handlers should be immunised against Hepatitis A, polio and typhoid, as per the frequency defined
by Department of Health, if not previously vaccinated.
Certificate of Fitness for Work
Upon the completion of the medical examination, certificate of fitness shall be issued by examining physician.
The certificate should respect the medical confidentiality and should not provide any information specifying
the clinical diagnosis or medical condition. The certificate should provide one of the following
recommendations:
a. Medically Fit for Job/location
b. Medically Unfit for Job/location
c. Temporarily Unfit for Job/location
d. AGMC decision
e. Others
Additional advice such as suitability for specified work duties (taking into account the job tasks/specifications)
and the job location, recommendations on any workplace adjustments, restrictions or precautionary measures
because of the existence of disease or disability, allowance for reduction of work hours where applicable, and
a time frame for subsequent follow-up and review, may also be provided on the fitness for work certificate.
For further details on fitness criteria, Contractors shall refer to latest ADNOC Group Medical Fitness
Guidelines.

MEDICAL PROVISIONS
Contractors and their Sub-contractors shall comply with all applicable UAE Laws, regulations and legal
requirements s with respect to medical provisions.

Contractors at their own cost and expense shall provide medical services (treatment, diagnostic investigations,
medical procedures, hospitalization, medicines or any other medical requirements) to their employees which
shall also include transportation for any request by their sick employees.

Contractors shall reimburse the employee all medical expenses associated with the contract as per the UAE
Labour Law.

Contractors shall refer to UAE Ministerial Order No. 37/2 of year 1982, Federal Law (8) of 1980, DOH Facility
licensing standards, OSHAD COP 4.0 & 5.0 and comply with the requirements there in and any amendments
thereto as applicable.

MEDICAL EMERGENCY PREPAREDNESS/PROVISIONS
Contractor shall ensure compliance with ADNOC and ADNOC Group Companies policies and procedures in
dealing with medical emergencies.
General
Contractors shall develop and implement Medical Emergency Response Plan, including medical evacuation
as part of the Facility Emergency Response Plan.
Contractors shall conduct emergency drills as per the established annual plan covering medical emergency
scenarios.
Contractors has the responsibility of coordinating medical emergency response to ensure seamless alignment
with surrounding industry and local emergency services.
Contractors shall have a written procedure on Medical Evacuation (MEDEVAC) from its operational location
to the nearest health service facility.
It is the responsibility of the Contractor representative to establish contacts with the nearest health support
services and have a clear chain of command for risk mitigation on-site.
The Medical Emergency Response procedure should at least contain the following:
▪ The list of trained First aiders
▪ Flow Chart of the process for medical response and evacuation
▪ The name and phone number of the ambulance services
▪ Names and contacts of the On-Scene Commanders
▪ Any other relevant information
ADNOC Group shall involve the contractor medical facilities and staff in its Emergency Preparedness
Exercises /Drills where deemed necessary.
MINIMUM FIRST AID AND MEDICAL STAFFING REQUIREMENTS
First Aid Requirements
First Aiders
First Aider must hold valid certificate from an accredited training provider as per Department of Health
(previously HAAD) standards.
Contractors whose number of workers does not exceed 50 workers in one place, on country or a circle of 20
km radius shall provide First aid kits at the workplace.
Ratios of first aiders to number of employees must be in line with the work place environment and needs of
the employer, employees and the requirements specific to the identified risks. The trained First aiders must
be identified and designated to the role and appropriately distributed in the work place.
Table 3 provides recommendation to the optimum number of first aiders to be present in addition to any
medically qualified professional in a work location.
Table 3 -Suggested numbers of First Aid personnel:
Category of Risk No of people employed No of first aid personnel needed
Low risk e.g. offices Fewer than 25 At least one appointed person
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 132 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
25-50 At least one trained first aid person
More than 50 At least one trained first aid person for every 100
people
Fewer than 5 At least one appointed person
5-50 At least one trained first aid person
Medium or High Risk
More than 50 At least one trained first aid person for every 50
people
Where there are In addition to the above, at least one first aider
hazards that require trained in the specific emergency action.
additional first aid skills
Refresher courses must be provided to ensure First Aiders have current and valid certification.
Nurse/Paramedic
An employer whose number of workers in one place or within an area the radius of which is twenty kilometres,
is exceeding fifty (50) workers but less than two hundred workers in one place, in addition to his obligation to
provide first aid kits, shall employ a nurse holding a nursing certificate recognized by the Ministry of Health
/DOH who shall be entrusted with rendering first aid treatment.
However, contractors are permitted to have a Service Level Agreement (SLA) with near-by contractors, to
share services of registered nurses/trained paramedics with prior approval from ADNOC Group.
Physician
Contractors shall appoint a Physician to treat workers in a place determined by him for this purpose and to
provide them with necessary drugs for treatment and all is free of charge (Article 2, 3, 4 & 5 of Ministerial
Resolution No. (37/2) of 1982 A.D on the medical care which the Contractors are obliged to provide to their
workers).
Contractors shall have a Service Level Agreement (SLA) with an accredited medical facility within 20 KM
radius and ensure the compliance with Department of Health access requirements (i.e. within 15 minutes
(urban) or 19 minutes (rural)) for provision of emergency medical services to cover all the work areas.
ADNOC Group in consultation with ADNOC Medical / HSE may request provision of the onsite physician
depending on the criticality of activities, camp / worksite location and number of employees.
First Aid Kit
Contractors shall provide first aid kit in an easily accessible location.
The designated First-aider should make sure that First-aid boxes are always well stocked and ready for use
in the case of an emergency.
All employees shall be made aware of the designated First Aiders in the workplace. First Aiders name and
telephone number should be displayed in the visible area.
Visible signage should be displayed identifying location of the First Aid Kit and CPR/AED Equipment. First Aid
kit contents should be finalized in consideration of the settings, scope of service and in reference to local &
Federal Regulatory Framework, Codes of Practice, international best practices and with due consideration on
the area and/or risk identified.
The First-aid kit shall be well stocked with sufficient items. An inventory of the First-aid kit should be displayed
near the First-aid box. The First-aid box contents should be checked regularly, at least every month, and
maintained on a regular basis by the designated First-aider. Particular note should be made to verify and
remove in time, items that are nearing their expiry date.
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 133 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
First-aider shall always ensure that the First-aid kit has clear instruction leaflets or guides available that can
be easily referred. It is always reassuring in the event of an emergency to have clear and easy instructions to
follow.
First Aid/Emergency Room
A suitable room e.g. sickbay, shall be made available for first aid purposes. Such rooms shall have sufficient
first aid resources be easily accessible to stretchers and be easily identifiable. Names and contact numbers
of emergency and/or first aid personnel shall be displayed on the door of the first aid room/sick bay.
AUTOMATED EXTERNAL DEFIBRILLATORS (AED)
(a) Employers should consider establishing an AED program and installing AEDs to manage sudden
cardiac arrest in their workplace, based on risk assessment and /or as mandated by relevant
authorities.
(b) An AED program should:
(i) Include a management system and written summary of the AED program, establishing clearly
defined lines of responsibility for those who oversee and monitor the program or participate
in it;
(ii) Assign a licensed health care professional for managing all medical aspects of the AED
program and oversee the program’s administration and coordination activities;
(iii) Be integrated in the more general plan describing emergency responses at the occupational
setting;
(iv) Include recognized and standardized training of all designated first aid responders and
employed healthcare professionals. Topics should include CPR and use of the specific AED
expected to be available and used at the occupational sit
Note: In case of sudden cardiac arrest, AEDs should be used by the first available suitably
trained first aider.
(v) Ensure selection and placement of AEDs and ancillary supplies that meet local and federal
legislation criteria for medical devices, and ensure they are regularly maintained as per the
manufacturer’s requirements; and
(vi) be incorporated into, or have its own quality assurance program.
(c) Placement of AEDs should be considered for all workplaces where people may congregate, such as
corporate offices, worksites, and employee camps/villages, in particular in the following areas:
(i) Areas where many people work closely together, such as assembly lines and office
buildings;
(ii) Close to a confined space;
(iii) Areas where electric-powered devices are used;
(iv) Outdoor worksites where lightning may occur;
(v) Health units where workers may seek treatment for heart attack symptoms;
(vi) Company fitness units and cafeterias; and
(vii) Remote sites, such as off-shore drilling rigs, construction projects, marine vessels, power
transmission lines, and energy pipe lines.
(d) AEDs should be conveniently installed and easily accessible to ensure response time within 3-5
minutes.
(e) The First-aider/ Certified Nurse/ Physician shall always record details of any injured person they treat.
It is important that the following information be written in a register:
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 134 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
▪ The name of the injured person and their occupation
▪ The nature of the injury
▪ A brief description of the treatment given and name of the person who gave the treatment
▪ How the accident occurred and what the injured person was doing at the time the accident
occurred
▪ Date, time and place of the accident
(f) Contractors shall display at the main gate used by workers to enter to the workplace / camp, the
following information:
▪ Location of the workers medical services
▪ Days, & working hours for providing the service
▪ Address of the hospital and specialist doctors who are entrusted to treat workers
▪ Information must be displayed in an easy manner for workers to see and in language understood
by all camp residents

MEANS OF TRANSPORTATION FOR MEDICAL EVACUATION (AMBULANCE)
Contractors shall make a necessary arrangement for the transportation of the ill or injured person to a suitable
health care facility.
Contractors shall demonstrate that an ambulance can reach to the scene of incident within the response time
of 19 minutes in rural areas and 15 minutes in urban areas. In addition, contractor shall ensure all the
components of the Emergency Response Plan are effectively implemented and tested on regular basis.
Provision of means of transportation (Ambulance) may not be required when the contractor works within the
radius of 20 km from any healthcare facilities complying to response time stated above where an established
service level agreement is avialble between Contractor and Healthcare Facility for Ambulance.
Contractors may be permitted to share means of transportation (Ambulance) services with neighbouring
contractors/facilities, provided there is a written Service Level Agreement (SLA) for such arrangement.
However, contractor shall ensure Ambulance can reach to the location of incident within 19 minutes response
time for rural and 15 minutes response time for urban areas. Contractor shall ensure strategic location for
stationing the ambulance, conduct Drills/Exercises to test and confirm that Ambulance can reach to incident
location within the preseribed response time.
Contractors shall ensure that the Ambulance shall be 4 WD while required to operate in remote locations and
also must possess CICPA pass as required.
Contractors shall ensure that medical facilities including ambulance availability for worksite as per UAE
Ministerial Order No. 37/2 of year 1982, Article 3 to improve the medical requirement/ ambulance availability
and comply with Department of Health (DOH) & OSHAD Standards as applicable.
All Ambulances and transfers shall conform to Department of Health (Previously HAAD) standards for
Minimum Requirements for the Preparedness of Common Medical Emergencies in Inpatient Care Setting,
Outpatient Care Setting, Ambulance Services and Inter facility Patient Transfer.
For non-life threatening injuries that require a higher level of medical treatment, the injured person can be
transported by a company vehicle to the nearest medical emergency treatment centre or hospital.
For serious injuries and injuries that are life threatening, ambulance services shall be used to transport the
patients to the nearest hospital for treatment.
ONSITE MEDICAL FACILITIES
Onsite Medical facilities shall be licensed by relevant UAE government health authorities – MOH / DOH
(previously HAAD) and shall comply with the minimum requirements set forth by those authorities (Since
standards are added and amended on frequent basis, it is advisable to check respective authority website for
the current requirements).
ADNOC Group shall have the right to audit the contractor’s medical facilities and records as required.
Contractors shall ensure medical wastes are disposed in line with relevant regulations through government
authorized agencies.
Medical Provision for WORKERS CAMPS /VILLAGES
Contractors who have workers camps/villages, or contract out to a third party to provide a workers
camp/village, shall ensure that:
i. First aid and/or medical emergency treatment facility is available onsite; and
ii. Ambulance services are provided
Document No: AHQ/HSE/HPG/GID/006/R01/20 Effective Date: 29/12/2020 Rev. No. 1
Page 137 of 152

ADNOC Classification: Internal
In addition to the requirements set above for first aid and medical emergency treatment, contractors shall
ensure that a community health education awareness program that provides the following services exists:
i. Education of employees/ workers on communicable diseases including blood borne pathogens, HIV,
Hepatitis B & C, sexually transmitted diseases (STD’s), chicken pox, and tuberculosis (TB)
ii. Drug and alcohol awareness campaigns
iii. Poison prevention campaign
iv. Smoking cessation campaigns
v. Vaccination & immunization program aligned with relevant HAAD policy and standards
vi. Education on healthy eating and basic nutrition
vii. Education on basic oral health and personal hygiene
viii. For workers exposed to heat in summer, a program of awareness and education regarding
precautions to be taken and the requirements of a heat stress management program
Contractors shall ensure that workers camps/ villages have a crisis management plan that addresses
potential outbreak of a contagious disease and meets requirements of relevant authorities, and that facilities
are designated to isolate employees that have contagious diseases (e.g., chicken pox, flu, etc.)
Contractors shall ensure workers camps/ villages have a medical emergency response plan that is tested, at
a minimum, annually.

APPENDIX 2: CONTRACTOR HSE PERFORMANCE MANAGEMENT MECHANISM
Objective
To ensure the engagement of the best performing Contractors across ADNOC Group, this HSE performance
management mechanism shall guide various stakeholders involved in the life cycle of contract management
to nurture positive HSE culture and address any issues proactively. Additionally, the mechanism entails
aspects  that  enable  ADNOC  Group  to  leverage  Contractor’s  engagement  through  motivation  and
consequence management approach.
Scope
This mechanism applies to and affect ADNOC Group Contractors who are undergoing bidders’ selection, pre-
contract award and during the execution phase.
Implementation
For implementation of HSE performance management mechanism, ADNOC Group concerned parties shall
refer to the Applicability Guidance Matrix and supplementary documents for managing Contractor’s HSE
performance. Guidance on applicability is provided in the subsequent sections of this appendix.
Contractor HSE Performance Evaluation is not mandated for ADNOC Group Companies that are contractually
contractors for other ADNOC Group Companies. However, Group Company at their discretion may conduct
HSE performance evaluation as deemed necessary to provide opportunity for improvements where required.
ADNOC and ADNOC Group Companies must conduct HSE performance evaluations for all of their respective
Contractors and submit reports as required.
Every contract under the jurisdiction of this mechanism shall be evaluated by Contract Administrator on a half-
yearly basis to determine HSE performance rating and to ensure that the results of evaluation are further
utilised in the procurement life cycle across ADNOC Group. Evaluation shall be conducted in line with the
following guidelines:

The outcome of the evaluation shall be reported to ADNOC Group concerned parties involved in contracting
activities, concerned Contractor and captured in the ADNOC Commercial Directory to be considered for the
future tendering process.
In the event of unsatisfactory HSE rating for any given contract within one ADNOC Group Company, that
particular Group Company may conduct a comprehensive audit on the Contractor’s other ongoing contracts,
to determine the level of consequence management that shall be applied.
When a Contractor providing services to multiple ADNOC Group Companies has unsatisfactory HSE rating in
one of the Group Company, the representatives from ADNOC and Group Companies may conduct a
comprehensive audit on the subject Contractor’s ongoing contracts, to determine the level of consequence
management that shall be applied.
For the single contract scope executed across several worksites, the following principles apply:
- The administrative authority over such contract lies with the Contract Administrator /Contract focal point
(who is the overall authority for contract execution); however, the User facility shall nominate a (non-HSE)
person to manage the contract execution at their respective worksites (i.e. supervising delivery, issuing
PTW, accounting man-hours worked, managing incidents, processing HSE non-compliances etc.)
- A single HSE Assurance Program shall be developed for such contract and implemented by all the User
facilities/ worksites.
- The supporting evidence for lagging indicators, Gates & HSE non-compliances shall be collected at all
User facilities and submitted to Contract Administrator (who is the overall authority for contract execution).
- Contract Administrator shall complete Contractor HSE Performance Evaluation report on a half-yearly
basis based on evidences received from all User facilities.
Where Contractor hires Sub-contractor(s) for part(s) of the scope delivered to ADNOC Group, any non-
compliances or incidents incurred by Sub-contractor will be accounted by ADNOC Group against the
respective Contractor. Hence, Contractor is accountable to integrate and engage their respective Sub-
contractors in effective management of HSE.
ADNOC Group Contract Administrators shall conduct verification of the Contractor HSE Assurance Program
and Contractor HSE Performance Management Mechanism implementation.
ADNOC Group Corporate HSE shall conduct desktop validation of minimum 10 % ABC type contracts in
addition to all low performing ABC contractors (<65% score achieved) and formally record the review to
confirm the effectiveness of the implementation.

* NOTES:
1)  The above listed items are for a guidance purpose and each Group Company could further enhance the list depending on the activities carried out by the
respective Group Company Contractors.
2)  Each non-compliance is subject to investigation by ADNOC Group Contract Administrator / Line HSE to apply deduction and consequences
3)  Not every eventuality could be covered within this list, therefore a sound judgment to be given case by case and upon investigation; a deduction shall be applied
along with the defined consequence process (action/action parties) as confirmed by ADNOC Group Corporate HSE Division.
4)  Actions detailed in Actions by ADNOC/ ADNOC Group Company (as relevant) column above are not required for CAC (Actual and potential severity 3 and
above) cases, and CAC reports forwarded to contractors is considered as case close out.

ATTACHMENT V TO APPENDIX 2: CONTRACTOR HSE ASSURANCE PROGRAM
Typical elements that shall be considered when developing a generic Contractor HSE Assurance Program for a
contract are mentioned below. The extent and description of the individual elements would depend on the
complexity of the contract scope and ADNOC Group Company operations/activities.
▪ Contractor Leadership Commitment
▪ Risk Management
▪ Transportation Safety
▪ HSE Training and Competency
▪ Medical Requirements
▪ Welfare Management
▪ Sub-contractor HSE Management
▪ Environmental Management
▪ ADNOC HSE Programs, HSE Awareness Campaigns
▪ Lessons Learned
▪ Emergency Response Preparedness
▪ Recognition and Accountability
• HSE Audits and Close Out
Contractors shall have an annual HSE Assurance program /HSE KPIs, follow ADNOC Group programs as
applicable, and integrate their Sub-contractors in this process.
ADNOC Group Contract Administrator shall conduct the Contractor HSE Assurance Program implementation
audit as per the established audit program.
